Preview activation reduced terminal evidence at 200k (`full 93511 -> 90230`, unique full `55594 -> 53926`, tail successes `38303 -> 36962`) and also reduced axis-quality attempts (`160286 -> 158182`). The extra forward check changed selected branches but did not add enough downstream controllability. Largest 200k improvements were `dense_sprint` seed 10 (`+65.97`), `opening_burst` seed 3 (`+62.18`), `drums_tide` seed 11 (`+62.14`), and `drums_crosscut` seed 4 (`+50.35`), but large regressions dominated: `verse_chorus` seed 3 (`-130.89`), `dense_sprint` seed 0 (`-95.92`), `dense_sprint` seed 3 (`-82.64`), `drums_crosscut` seed 7 (`-62.22`), `opening_burst` seed 1 (`-60.17`), and `drums_signature` seed 5 (`-56.19`). Forward awareness is still a good direction, but this implementation is too expensive and branch-churny as a global quality-search preview.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-semantic-reserve-85` at commit `100c618`.
- Hypothesis: Near-tail speculative completion still spends heavily on duplicate-prone `remaining=1..2` completions, where ordinary DFS is already close to a full leaf and improvement yield is low. Throttle only those shallow completions after a passing output exists, using smooth scalar-budget pressure and unique-full-feedback maturity, so low-budget/pre-validity completion remains intact while mature quality search frees terminal work for higher-yield futures.
- Code changes made: Added a quality-phase shallow-tail throttle for `remainingContacts <= 2`. The throttle pressure is `smoothstep(budget / (budget + 150000)) * smoothstep(unique_full / (unique_full + 24))`, capped at `1.0` and applied with a deterministic node hash. `shouldAttemptNearTailCompletion` now receives `qualitySearch` and telemetry; pre-validity tail completion remains unchanged. Candidate generation, validation gates, ranking/scoring, tail window size, suffix repair, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: ACCEPT`; baseline `447.0` -> candidate `447.1`; `Δheadline = +0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 0.2]`, `P(Δ<=0)=7.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.0`, `200k=+0.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The mechanism did exactly the targeted work-allocation shift at 200k: tail attempts fell `50897 -> 24409`, tail successes `38303 -> 24409`, duplicate full evaluations `37917 -> 24093`, and unique full evaluations rose `55594 -> 56101`. Tail best yield improved (`1347/38303 -> 1248/24409`), with `remaining=1` attempts roughly halved (`24658 -> 13213`) and `remaining=2` attempts also reduced (`11444 -> 5768`). Row churn was small: largest 200k improvements were `cold_start` seed 8 (`+15.81`), `drums_signature` seed 10 (`+11.43`), `rhythm_ladder` seed 4 (`+7.38`), and `drums_crescendo` seed 9 (`+1.03`); largest regressions were `cold_start` seed 1 (`-3.89`) and `drums_pendulum` seed 2 (`-2.27`). This is a narrow accepted terminal-conversion improvement, not a solution to the persistent low-air/speed placement ceiling.
- Status: Kept and committed as the new baseline.

## arc-budget-low-air-speed-drag-90

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: The worst 200k rows still have simultaneous low-air and overspeed error. The existing air-axis quality stream uses an `air_support` sample that adds little selected coverage, so swap that stream to a physically braking `speed_drag` future for low-air targets and activate it only through smooth scalar-budget pressure and smooth full-feedback maturity. This should give mature low-air rows speed-bleeding futures without adding a broad always-on family.
- Code changes made: Temporarily replaced the air-axis quality stream's one `air_support` sample with two `speed_drag` samples, using `budgetScaleFrames=150000` and `fullFeedbackScale=48`. Candidate validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-low-air-speed-drag-90`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `446.3`; `Δheadline = -0.7`, 95% CI `[-3.3, 0.6]`, `P(Δ<=0)=78.7%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-1.0`, `150k=-0.6`, `200k=-1.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream did not create useful terminal diversity: at 200k full evaluations were essentially flat (`80194 -> 80148`), unique full evaluations were flat (`56101 -> 56095`), and duplicate full evaluations fell only because fewer tail completions ran (`24093 -> 24053`). It displaced the existing air-axis stream instead of improving it: air axis-quality attempts/successes fell `4428/1341 -> 2576/790`, and selected air-axis quality candidates fell `12 -> 2`. Aggregate 200k axis MAE moved slightly against speed (`0.2107 -> 0.2117`) while air improved only slightly (`0.1078 -> 0.1074`). Largest 200k gains were `drums_crescendo` seed 11 (`+52.33`), `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+14.11`), `cold_start` seed 8 (`+1.57`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 2 (`+0.88`); largest 200k regressions were `drums_pendulum` seed 10 (`-115.58`), `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`-40.30`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 2 (`-20.75`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 3 (`-18.12`), and `syncopated_switchback` seed 0 (`-16.43`). For the largest regression, the candidate reduced mean air error (`0.267 -> 0.231`) but increased mean speed error (`0.244 -> 0.397`) and speed max error (`0.459 -> 1.582`), so local braking did not leave a controllable downstream state.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-air-support-mode-91

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: The declared `air_support` sampling mode is effectively normal geometry today, and the baseline air-axis stream is low-yield. Make `air_support` physically distinct by adding stable low-air support controls, but meter the existing one-sample stream with smooth scalar-budget pressure and smooth full-feedback maturity so the new support future appears gradually rather than as a fixed knob.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `airSupportModePressure` to target-state placement controls. The mode reduced contact jitter, modestly increased low-air post-contact support length/floor/cap, slightly shortened pre-contact support, and added only a small overspeed-linked speed-control term. The existing air-axis stream kept one sample but gained `budgetScaleFrames=150000` and `fullFeedbackScale=48`. Candidate validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-air-support-mode-91`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `446.6`; `Δheadline = -0.4`, 95% CI `[-3.1, 1.2]`, `P(Δ<=0)=62.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=-0.3`, `150k=-0.2`, `200k=-0.8`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The budget/maturity metering worked mechanically and improved terminal evidence slightly at 200k (`full 80194 -> 80391`, unique full `56101 -> 56250`), while reducing low-yield air-support work (`air axis-quality attempts/successes 4428/1341 -> 1233/450`, selected air axis-quality `12 -> 6`). That work-allocation gain did not convert to score: aggregate row quality still lost at 100k-200k, with branch churn concentrated in a few rows. Largest 200k gains were `drums_crescendo` seed 11 (`+52.33`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 11 (`+48.38`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 9 (`+22.46`), and `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+14.11`); largest regressions were `drums_pendulum` seed 10 (`-115.58`), `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`-40.30`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 4 (`-31.84`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 3 (`-29.65`), and `syncopated_switchback` seed 2 (`-20.00`). This suggests the smooth metering is appropriate, but the support geometry still changes branch choice without reliably improving downstream controllability.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-air-stream-meter-92

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 91 showed a small terminal-feedback gain but mixed it with new support geometry. Isolate the scheduler part: keep `air_support` geometry exactly as the accepted baseline, but meter the existing one-sample low-air stream with smooth scalar-budget pressure and smooth full-feedback maturity. This should remove low-yield air-axis work without introducing new branch geometry.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `budgetScaleFrames=150000` and `fullFeedbackScale=48` to the existing air-axis `air_support` stream only. Candidate geometry, validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-air-stream-meter-92`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `446.4`; `Δheadline = -0.6`, 95% CI `[-3.2, 0.6]`, `P(Δ<=0)=75.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-1.0`, `150k=-0.4`, `200k=-0.9`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The metering cut air-axis work sharply (`air axis-quality attempts/successes 4428/1341 -> 1233/370`, selected air axis-quality `12 -> 1`) but did not free useful terminal evidence: at 200k full evaluations were effectively unchanged (`80194 -> 80182`), unique full barely moved (`56101 -> 56111`), and tail improvements fell (`1248 -> 1242`). Largest 200k gains were `drums_crescendo` seed 11 (`+52.33`), `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+14.11`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 2 (`+0.88`); largest regressions were `drums_pendulum` seed 10 (`-115.58`), `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`-40.30`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 2 (`-20.75`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 3 (`-18.12`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 1 (`-12.61`). This argues the baseline's small air-support stream is not the bottleneck; removing it changes branch ordering enough to hurt without adding better downstream futures.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-start-option-breadth-93

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: The recurring hard rows are highly sensitive to start choice and early cadence. Keep start scoring and all per-gap geometry unchanged, but allow a deterministic, smoothly budget-scaled number of extra already-scored start roots into the frontier at larger budgets. This should expose different early physical futures without changing candidate formulas or scoring.
- Code changes made: Temporarily passed `targetBudget` into `buildStartOptions` and added `budgetAwareStartOptionLimit`, which added `0..3` extra start options using `smoothstep(budget / (budget + 200000))` and a seed-stable fractional hash. The start scoring pool remained `16`; only the number of scored starts returned to the search increased. Candidate geometry, validation, ranking/scoring, start feasibility scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-start-option-breadth-93`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `445.1`; `Δheadline = -2.0`, 95% CI `[-7.6, 4.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=75.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.1`, `100k=-1.5`, `150k=-1.8`, `200k=-3.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The mechanism changed many early futures: start options rose from `10.0` per row to `10.8/11.3/11.8` at 100k/150k/200k. It did improve some rows substantially, but broad branch churn dominated and terminal diversity worsened at 200k (`full 80194 -> 77456`, unique full `56101 -> 54081`). Axis-quality attempts rose (`161313 -> 164504`) while full terminal feedback fell, so extra starts starved later conversion. Largest 200k gains were `syncopated_switchback` seed 8 (`+111.94`), `dense_sprint` seed 8 (`+93.83`), `verse_chorus` seed 2 (`+93.03`), `drums_crescendo` seed 0 (`+80.85`), and `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+78.34`); largest regressions were `verse_chorus` seed 8 (`-130.00`), `dense_sprint` seed 0 (`-95.92`), `drums_crosscut` seed 0 (`-95.14`), `verse_chorus` seed 10 (`-88.71`), `rhythm_ladder` seed 1 (`-84.68`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 4 (`-71.48`). This says globally broadening starts is too expensive and unstable; any start mechanism would need a much narrower scarcity/weakness gate.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-mid-tail-throttle-94

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted shallow-tail throttle removed duplicate-prone completions for `remaining<=2`, but baseline diagnostics still show low best/success yield at remaining `3..4` compared with deeper `8..9` completions. Extend the same smooth budget/full-feedback throttle with a much lower depth-tapered pressure for `remaining=3..4`, leaving deeper high-yield tail completions unchanged.
- Code changes made: Temporarily changed the quality-phase near-tail throttle gate from `remaining<=2` to `remaining<=4`. The existing depth `1..2` pressure stayed unchanged; `remaining=3` used at most `0.30` of the existing pressure and `remaining=4` at most `0.15`. Candidate generation, validation, ranking/scoring, tail window size, suffix repair, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-mid-tail-throttle-94`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `447.1`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were all `+0.0` after paired rounding, and validity was unchanged at every budget. The throttle did change work allocation: at 200k tail attempts/successes fell `33833/24409 -> 33006/23852`, duplicate full evaluations fell `24093 -> 23541`, and unique full evaluations rose slightly `56101 -> 56183`. Remaining-3 attempts fell (`5587 -> 4828`) while remaining-5+ depths stayed effectively unchanged. No 200k row scores changed, so the extra terminal diversity did not convert into score. This is a useful diagnostic but not a promotable mechanism under the canonical decision rule.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-speed-carry-stream-95

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: Existing speed semantic streams mostly create brake/drag futures, while several weak rows show sustained underspeed or mixed speed error after prior braking selections. Add a structurally distinct `speed_carry` placement family that appears only in mature quality search under smooth scalar-budget, unique-full-feedback, and current underspeed pressure. The family should offer downhill/carrying exits without changing contract search, validation, or scoring.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `speed_carry` to candidate sample modes, added one current-speed axis-quality stream with underspeed activation, target bias `+0.14`, `budgetScaleFrames=180000`, and `fullFeedbackScale=48`, generalized axis-quality target bias to current-target policies, and added carry geometry controls that raised contact/post angles, shortened pre-contact support slightly, and lengthened post-contact support/cap. Candidate validation, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-speed-carry-stream-95`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `446.9`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.4, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-0.2`, `150k=+0.1`, `200k=-0.3`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The family was mechanically active but poorly landable: at 200k it added `5610` `speed_carry` samples with only `450` direct landings, while preclear rejections rose by `3097`. Normal/brake/speed-drag samples fell (`-1571/-549/-230`), viable candidates fell by `260`, and selected axis-quality candidates fell by `3`, so the stream displaced better futures instead of adding usable coverage. Largest 200k improvement was only `drums_tide` seed 1 (`+1.43`), while regressions included `drums_signature` seed 5 (`-60.93`), `dense_sprint` seed 7 (`-6.72`), `drums_crescendo` seed 2 (`-6.23`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-5.04`). The core direction may still be useful, but this version is too aggressive and fails at approach/landing coverage.
- Status: Rejected; not committed. Revised into the more landable `arc-budget-landable-speed-carry-96` attempt before deciding whether to drop the family.

## arc-budget-landable-speed-carry-96

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 95 tested a useful missing family, but its biased/aggressive carry geometry had too many preclear failures. Keep the same budget-aware underspeed family, but remove current-target bias and make the approach more landable: lower contact jitter, shorter pre-contact support, a small preclear contribution, and a less extreme downhill/carry exit.
- Code changes made: Temporarily kept the `speed_carry` candidate sample mode and one budget/full-feedback-scaled current-speed underspeed stream, but removed the `+0.14` target bias. The geometry controls were softened: contact/post angle boosts were reduced, contact jitter was reduced instead of increased, pre-contact support was shortened more strongly, and post-contact length/floor/cap boosts were reduced. Candidate validation, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-landable-speed-carry-96`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `447.0`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.9, 0.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=57.5%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-0.1`, `150k=-0.1`, `200k=-0.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. Landability improved only modestly and still did not convert to score: at 200k the family produced `3018` samples, `223` direct landings, and `1682` preclear rejections. Aggregate 200k viable candidates rose by `466`, but full/unique full evaluations fell (`-56/-40`) and tail attempts/successes fell (`-49/-13`). Largest 200k gain was `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`+37.50`), but regressions offset it, especially `syncopated_switchback` seed 4 (`-40.54`), plus `cold_start` seed 1 (`-2.58`) and `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`-1.02`). The carry family reduced the blast radius versus attempt 95, but it still adds low-yield candidates and does not improve canonical quality.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Dropped the speed-carry family for now.

## arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-shallow-tail-throttle-89` at commit `11e5469`.
- Hypothesis: Reuse candidates are already high-yield physically plausible futures because each translates a prior validated catch shape to the current rider state and revalidates it in the engine. Offer a second recent reuse shape only in mature quality search, using smooth scalar-budget pressure and smooth unique-full-feedback pressure, so larger budgets can buy more stable/periodic downstream states without changing geometry, scoring, or contract search.
- Code changes made: Added a deterministic budget/full-feedback scheduler for one extra reuse slot in quality search. Contract search remains at `HANDOFF_REUSE_K=1`; quality search keeps one reuse candidate by default and adds a second only when `unitHash(node)` falls under `0.35 * smoothstep(budget/(budget+150000)) * smoothstep(unique_full/(unique_full+48))`. The reuse cache is keyed by the selected reuse limit so deterministic per-node validation is preserved. Candidate generation formulas, validation gates, scoring, axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix eligibility, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: ACCEPT`; baseline `447.1` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.8`, 95% CI `[-1.0, 2.5]`, `P(Δ<=0)=17.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.5`, `150k=+0.4`, `200k=+1.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The mechanism shifted mature rows toward additional reuse selections (`+10/+28/+53` selected reuse at 100k/150k/200k), reduced duplicate full evaluations at 200k (`-253`), and improved many rows, including at 200k: `drums_swell` seed 7 (`+65.80`), `drums_dropout` seed 6 (`+61.49`), `opening_burst` seed 3 (`+60.06`), `dense_sprint` seed 8 (`+57.30`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`+54.67`), and `drums_pulse` seed 0 (`+44.34`). The tradeoff is real branch churn: 200k full/unique full evaluations fell (`-379/-126`) and tail attempts/successes fell (`-401/-252`), with notable 200k regressions in `drums_zigzag` seed 7 (`-101.41`), `opening_burst` seed 10 (`-70.66`), `drums_swell` seed 5 (`-42.52`), and `drums_breath` seed 9 (`-41.82`). Despite churn, the paired canonical decision accepts the smoother mature reuse breadth.
- Status: Kept and committed as the new baseline.

## arc-budget-duplicate-aware-reuse-98

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted second reuse slot improves mature budgets but is branch-churny. Since reuse can collapse repeated terminal basins, gate the extra reuse slot by a smooth duplicate-full ratio in addition to budget and unique-full maturity. This should preserve reuse breadth where the row is duplicating terminal outputs while reducing it where terminal coverage is still scarce.
- Code changes made: Temporarily multiplied `matureReuseExtraPressure` by `smoothstep((duplicate_full / (unique_full + duplicate_full)) / 0.30)`. The existing budget pressure, unique-full pressure, deterministic node hash, reuse cache keying, geometry, validation, scoring, axis-quality streams, contract search, start options, tail/suffix eligibility, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-duplicate-aware-reuse-98`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.7`; `Δheadline = -0.2`, 95% CI `[-1.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.2`, `200k=-0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The gate mostly removed useful accepted reuse work rather than avoiding bad churn. At 150k reuse attempts/successes fell by `242/126`, selected reuse fell by `5`, full/unique full evaluations fell by `85/55`, and the only changed score was `opening_burst` seed 3 losing the accepted gain (`-60.06`). At 200k reuse attempts/successes fell by `754/361`, selected reuse fell by `4`, full/unique full fell by `88/63`, and changed scores were again `opening_burst` seed 3 (`-60.06`) plus a negligible `solo_run` seed 0 (`-0.02`). Duplicate-full ratio is not the right guard for this mechanism.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-reuse-normal-reserve-99

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted mature reuse slot adds useful futures but increases per-node breadth. Rather than gating reuse off, keep the extra reuse candidate and reserve one ordinary normal quality candidate whenever that extra reuse slot is active. This should preserve the new physically meaningful reuse future while reducing normal-sample churn and freeing budget for terminal conversion.
- Code changes made: Temporarily computed the reuse limit before normal candidate sampling in `rankedOptions`, passed the extra reuse count into `scarceFeedbackQualityNormalCandidateCount`, and subtracted one normal candidate whenever the mature extra reuse slot was active. Contract search, reuse scheduling pressure, reuse cache keying, geometry, validation, scoring, axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix eligibility, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-reuse-normal-reserve-99`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.8`; `Δheadline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-1.4, 1.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=51.2%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.7`, `150k=-0.7`, `200k=+0.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The reserve did reduce some ordinary work but caused unstable row churn. At 100k it helped `drums_crescendo` seed 6 (`+160.00`) and `drums_zigzag` seed 11 (`+17.39`) while hurting `drums_breath` seed 5 (`-33.06`). At 150k the losses dominated: `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`-156.98`), `drums_breath` seed 5 (`-44.46`), and `grain_staircase` seed 8 (`-39.90`) outweighed gains like `dense_sprint` seed 3 (`+79.54`) and `cold_start` seed 1 (`+32.03`). At 200k the signal was mixed: gains included `drums_tide` seed 7 (`+44.98`) and `drums_crescendo` seed 1 (`+33.42`), while losses included `grain_staircase` seed 3 (`-31.55`), `verse_chorus` seed 2 (`-18.31`), and `drums_crescendo` seed 9 (`-15.00`). Reserving normal breadth for reuse is not stable enough.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-release-vertical-setup-100

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Remaining weak rows are speed/air dominated, and a candidate can land the current contact while leaving a launchy vertical release state that makes the next tight/low-air contact hard to control. Add release vertical velocity metadata and a small, smooth budget/full-feedback-scaled quality ranking term that penalizes excessive absolute vertical release speed before tight or low-air next contacts. This selects among already engine-validated candidates; it does not alter geometry or validation gates.
- Code changes made: Temporarily populated `Detection.measurements.velocity` in the candidate-window detector, added `releaseVelocityY` to `GapFit`, carried it through candidate evaluation and snapshot cloning, and extended `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty` with a vertical term. The vertical term used `smoothstep(budget/(budget+150000)) * smoothstep(unique_full/(unique_full+48))`, next-contact low-air/cadence pressure, a safe vertical speed from `8` down to `4` px/frame, and weight `0.035`. Existing release-speed setup, candidate generation, validation gates, scoring metric, axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-release-vertical-setup-100`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.0`; `Δheadline = +0.1`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=34.1%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.1`, `200k=+0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The effect was tiny and clean but below the accept threshold. At 150k only one row changed: `drums_tide` seed 7 improved `+22.62`, while aggregate full/unique full evaluations rose `+38/+33`. At 200k only two rows changed, both gains: `opening_burst` seed 10 (`+34.03`) and `drums_tide` seed 7 (`+13.57`); aggregate full/unique full rose `+136/+120`, tail successes rose `+19`, and viable candidates rose `+450` despite fewer total samples. The mechanism is directionally plausible but not accepted by canonical decide.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.4`, `200k=+0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The behavior was smooth and localized but did not convert broadly enough. At 150k only two rows changed, both `drums_pendulum`: seed 4 improved `+46.12` and seed 9 improved `+21.77`, with suffix attempts/successes/improvements/nodes rising by `+3/+3/+2/+141`. The extra attempts help some suffix-completable pendulum rows but mostly spend extra work in the same narrow basin.

## arc-budget-grounded-support-102

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The weakest mature rows show early low-air misses where the rider lands the contact but does not stay grounded enough. Add a separate low-air `grounded_support` proposal stream, activated smoothly by scalar budget, unique-full-feedback maturity, and early/tight-cadence pressure, while leaving the existing `air_support` stream unchanged. The new geometry should shorten risky pre-contact support, reduce contact jitter, and lengthen/brake post-contact grounded support.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `grounded_support` to candidate sample modes, added an air-axis quality stream with `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, start/cadence temporal pressure, and added grounded-support geometry controls in target-state placement. Candidate validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged. The first implementation contained a local initialization bug: the grounded-support pressure referenced `dense` before `dense` was initialized.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-grounded-support-102`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `221.4`; `Δheadline = -226.5`, 95% CI `[-314.4, -72.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: This archive is a runtime-failure diagnostic, not a valid mechanism read. The smooth budget/full-feedback gate kept 25k and 50k byte-equivalent, but at 100k+ the stream activated and rows that sampled it threw `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'dense' before initialization`. Validity collapsed to `91%/88%/84%` at `100k/150k/200k`, with 200k score `185.3`. The bug was fixed in the follow-up attempt before judging the mechanism itself.
- Status: Rejected and superseded by fixed attempt `arc-budget-grounded-support-103`; not committed.

## arc-budget-grounded-support-103

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Same as attempt 102, with the grounded-support geometry pressure computed after the cadence-density signal exists. The fixed stream should provide a budget-matured, physically distinct low-air grounded-support future without destabilizing low budgets.
- Code changes made: Same temporary `grounded_support` sample mode, air-axis stream, temporal stream pressure, and target-state grounded-support geometry controls as attempt 102, but with the local `dense` initialization order fixed. Candidate validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, contract search, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-grounded-support-103`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.7`; `Δheadline = -0.2`, 95% CI `[-0.9, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-0.2`, `150k=-0.2`, `200k=-0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream was active but low-yield: at 200k it added `815` grounded-support samples, with `251` direct landings and `402` preclear rejections. Air axis-quality attempts/successes rose by `+885/+257`, but selected axis-quality candidates fell by `1`, full/unique full evaluations fell by `-193/-147`, and tail improvements fell by `-4`. Only two rows changed at 200k, both losses: `syncopated_switchback` seed 4 (`-36.43`) and `cold_start` seed 8 (`-15.81`). The proposed family broadened candidate coverage mechanically but did not add selected downstream futures, and it starved a small amount of better terminal conversion.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-air-support-additive-104

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The existing low-air `air_support` stream is small and occasionally useful, but counting it in the normal-candidate reserve may displace ordinary/stronger speed futures in mature quality search. Keep the stream and its geometry unchanged, but make it additive for reserve accounting; the existing reserve pressure still scales smoothly with budget and unique-full feedback for the remaining semantic streams.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `reserveNormalBudget?: boolean` to axis-quality stream policy, set the current `air_support` stream to `reserveNormalBudget: false`, and skipped such policies in `estimateAxisQualitySampleCount`. Candidate generation, stream eligibility, validation gates, ranking/scoring, tail/suffix scheduling, diagnostics, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-air-support-additive-104`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were effectively flat: `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.0`, `200k=+0.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The mechanism was effectively a no-op on the current accepted baseline. Only one scored row changed, `cold_start` seed 6 at 50k (`-3.85`). At 200k no row scores changed; aggregate air axis-quality attempts/successes fell by `-42/-13`, full/unique full evaluations moved by `+1/-5`, and tail successes rose by `+6`, none of which converted into quality.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-reuse-fill-105

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted second reuse slot works because reused catches are already physically validated futures, but the current reuse scan counts attempted prior fits, not successful translated candidates. At mature budgets, keep the offered reuse candidate count unchanged while scanning a few more prior validated catches to fill those slots when recent translations fail. This should improve reuse coverage without adding raw geometry or increasing ranked reuse breadth.
- Code changes made: Temporarily split reuse cache keying into offered reuse count and scan-attempt count. Quality search kept the existing `reuseCandidateLimit`, then added up to two extra prior-fit scan attempts using `0.45 * smoothstep(budget/(budget+150000)) * smoothstep(unique_full/(unique_full+48))` and a node-stable fractional hash. `reuseCatchCandidates` stopped after the original offered-candidate count was filled, so contract search, validation gates, scoring, candidate generation formulas, axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-reuse-fill-105`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `446.5`; `Δheadline = -1.3`, 95% CI `[-4.2, 0.5]`, `P(Δ<=0)=90.8%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-0.3`, `150k=-1.9`, `200k=-2.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The mechanism mechanically increased reuse coverage but over-churned selected futures. At 200k reuse attempts/successes rose by `+12411/+2055`, selected reuse rose `+14`, selected pool fell `-55`, selected brake rose `+44`, full/unique full fell `-498/-175`, and tail successes fell `-313` while tail improvements rose only `+5`. It did help several rows, including `rhythm_ladder` seed 8 (`+43.66`), `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+40.31`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+40.27`), `opening_burst` seed 0 (`+38.91`), and `drums_tide` seed 3 (`+32.30`), but high-scoring regressions dominated: `verse_chorus` seed 3 (`-144.29`), `drums_pulse` seed 0 (`-74.27`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`-74.09`), `drums_breath` seed 11 (`-71.98`), `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-57.68`), and `dense_sprint` seed 11 (`-52.91`). Filling reuse slots from older catches is too broad without a stronger quality/weakness gate.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-release-vertical-mature-106

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Release-state forward awareness remains the cleanest positive signal from the previous diagnostics. Expose candidate release vertical velocity and add a smooth mature-quality ranking penalty for excessive absolute vertical release velocity before low-air or tight next contacts. The pressure should scale with scalar budget and unique-full feedback so cheap budgets keep broad contact coverage while mature rows can prefer less launchy exits.
- Code changes made: Temporarily populated candidate-window velocity samples, added `releaseVelocityY` to `GapFit`, carried it through candidate evaluation and snapshot cloning, and added a release-vertical setup term to `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty`. The term used next low-air pressure, tight-cadence pressure, `smoothstep(budget/(budget+150000))`, `smoothstep(unique_full/(unique_full+48))`, a safe vertical speed ramp from `8` to `5` px/frame, and weight `0.06`. Candidate geometry, validation gates, real scoring targets, axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-release-vertical-mature-106`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were all `+0.0`, and validity was unchanged at every budget. No scored checkpoint changed score/status, and no track hash changed. Only nine checkpoints had tiny compile-counter differences, such as small changes in sampled/viable candidates, full evaluations, or reuse/brake/axis-quality attempts, with no selected track effect. The smooth budget-aware ranking pressure was mechanically present but either too weak or redundant with existing ordering; it did not add or select new downstream futures.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-next-air-support-107

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Current low-air support only targets the contact being placed. Add a tiny forward-aware proposal stream that, when the next contact asks for low air, shapes the current catch using the next air target. The stream should activate smoothly with scalar budget and unique-full feedback, preserving cheap-budget breadth while letting mature rows offer support futures that make the next low-air beat more catchable.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added one `air_support` axis-quality stream with `targetSource: "next"`, `targetMax=0.35`, `samples=1`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and a distinct deterministic seed/attempt offset. Existing current-air support remained unchanged. Candidate geometry formulas, engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-next-air-support-107`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.5, 0.5]`, `P(Δ<=0)=52.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.0`, `200k=-0.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream was mechanically active: at 200k air-axis attempts/successes rose `4494/1344 -> 6700/1945`, selected air-axis candidates rose `10 -> 16`, and full/unique full evaluations ticked up `79815/55975 -> 79833/55997`. It added useful local futures for `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+32.10` at 200k), `drums_pendulum` seed 11 (`+26.49`), `drums_pendulum` seed 8 (`+18.08`), and `cold_start` seed 10 (`+4.88`). Losses offset the gains: `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`-40.63`), `drums_dropout` seed 6 (`-25.75`), `drums_dropout` seed 4 (`-14.93`), `drums_pendulum` seed 3 (`-9.56`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-5.04`). The mechanism broadens forward-air coverage, but `targetMax=0.35` is too broad and causes branch churn without net canonical improvement.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-next-air-low-pressure-108

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 107 was structurally useful but too blunt because all next-air targets up to `0.35` got full stream pressure. Keep the forward-aware next-air support idea, but replace the hard low-air target threshold with a smooth low-target pressure, so very low next-air beats receive most of the stream while moderate low-air beats receive only a fractional chance.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `lowTargetScale` to axis-quality stream policies and made air-stream pressure `smoothstep((scale - target) / scale)` when that field is present. The next-air support stream used `samples=1`, `targetSource: "next"`, `lowTargetScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and the same deterministic seed/attempt offset as attempt 107. Existing current-air support, speed streams, candidate geometry formulas, engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-next-air-low-pressure-108`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.0`; `Δheadline = +0.2`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 0.7]`, `P(Δ<=0)=25.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.1`, `200k=+0.4`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The smoother pressure avoided the broad 107 churn: at 200k only six rows changed, with gains in `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+32.10`), `drums_pendulum` seed 11 (`+26.49`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+19.83`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`+4.57`), and losses in `drums_pendulum` seed 3 (`-9.56`) and `cold_start` seed 2 (`-4.63`). Work shifted modestly: 200k air-axis attempts/successes rose `4494/1344 -> 5569/1581`, selected air-axis candidates rose `10 -> 13`, full/unique full evaluations rose `+54/+39`, and tail successes rose `+12`. Directionally useful, but the paired canonical decision did not accept it.
- Status: Not kept as-is; revised into the broader two-sample follow-up `arc-budget-next-air-low-pressure-109`.

## arc-budget-next-air-low-pressure-109

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 108 had clean positive local signal but may have been under-sampling the forward-air setup future. Keep the same smooth low-target, budget, and feedback pressure, but offer two deterministic proposal samples in the stream so mature rows can see a meaningfully different second low-air setup geometry without changing cheap-budget behavior.

- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.0`; `Δheadline = +0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.3, 0.6]`, `P(Δ<=0)=39.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.1`, `200k=+0.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The extra proposal did not improve the 108 signal. It preserved some gains such as `drums_pendulum` seed 0 and `drums_dropout` seed 2, and newly helped `drums_pendulum` seed 2, but it also reintroduced broader branch churn, notably pulling `drums_crescendo` seed 7 back down from the 108 no-change behavior. Add a separate next-low-air `speed_drag` proposal stream, activated with the same smooth low-target, budget, and feedback pressure, so mature rows can see a physically braking setup candidate before very low-air next beats.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `lowTargetScale` to axis-quality stream policies, added smooth low-air target pressure for air policies, and registered one next-air `speed_drag` stream with `targetSource: "next"`, `lowTargetScale=0.45`, `samples=1`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and a distinct seed/attempt offset. Existing current-air support, speed streams, candidate geometry formulas, engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.

The stream changed very few scored rows: at 200k `syncopated_switchback` seed 11 gained `+37.59`, `drums_pendulum` seed 5 gained `+0.33`, and `cold_start` seed 2 lost `-4.63`. The drag setup did not help the main pendulum low-air/speed failures and spent work less efficiently than the sparse support stream.

## arc-budget-speed-settle-mode-111

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The current mature deep-speed settle stream uses abrupt `speed_drag` geometry. Add a distinct `speed_settle` candidate family for that already budget/full-feedback-gated stream: longer, milder uphill post-contact support should physically bleed speed while staying more stable than drag, exposing controllable low-speed futures without changing stream scheduling.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `speed_settle` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added `speedSettleModePressure` to target-state placement controls, and switched only the existing `HANDOFF_NEXT_SPEED_DEEP_SETTLE` stream from `speed_drag` to `speed_settle`. The new mode used lower landing uncertainty than drag, modest extra preclear pressure, milder contact/pre/post angle reductions, longer post-contact support/floor/cap, and the existing smooth deep-settle budget/full-feedback stream schedule. Engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-speed-settle-mode-111`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.8`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-3.1, 2.8]`, `P(Δ<=0)=51.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=-0.9`, `150k=+0.9`, `200k=-0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The family was mechanically active and landable: at 200k it sampled `9609` `speed_settle` candidates with `3571` direct landings and `502` preclear rejections, while full/unique full evaluations rose `+1150/+1119`. The problem was branch churn. Big 200k gains included `verse_chorus` seed 6 (`+86.16`), `drums_zigzag` seed 7 (`+68.95`), `grain_staircase` seed 4 (`+66.54`), `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+63.51`), and `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+62.31`), but losses dominated: `dense_sprint` seed 0 (`-94.30`), `mini_burst` seed 6 (`-82.73`), `rhythm_ladder` seed 4 (`-73.36`), `mini_burst` seed 5 (`-72.96`), `dense_sprint` seed 8 (`-65.91`), `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-65.67`), `verse_chorus` seed 0 (`-65.18`), and `drums_dropout` seed 6 (`-54.96`). The mode creates plausible futures, but replacing the deep-settle stream geometry globally is too disruptive.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-weak-next-air-support-112

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The cleanest prior generator-side signal was the sparse next-low-air support stream, but it still caused some branch churn. Make that stream smoothly budget-aware, full-feedback-aware, low-target-aware, and incumbent-weakness-aware, so it only adds forward-air setup candidates when the current best passing output still has weak axis quality.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `lowTargetScale`, `weakBestAxisQuality`, and `weakBestAxisQualityWidth` policy fields; added one next-air `air_support` stream with `samples=1`, `targetSource: "next"`, `lowTargetScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, weak-best threshold `0.48`, and width `0.20`; passed `LeafKey` best-so-far state into quality candidate generation, normal-candidate reserve estimates, tail completion, and suffix repair; and added a best-axis-quality bucket to the per-node axis-quality cache key. Candidate geometry formulas, engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, reuse, brake streams, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-next-air-support-112`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `446.5`; `Δheadline = -1.4`, 95% CI `[-3.2, -0.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=98.2%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=-0.0`, `50k=-0.5`, `100k=-3.2`, `150k=-1.0`, `200k=-1.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The intended weak next-air stream was too entangled with cache invalidation: at 200k air axis-quality attempts/successes rose only `4494/1344 -> 4774/1397`, but speed axis-quality attempts/successes rose `155376/67513 -> 162718/70890`, full/unique full evaluations fell `79815/55975 -> 77481/54820`, tail successes fell `24157 -> 23417`, and selected axis-quality candidates fell `1693 -> 1677`. Largest 200k gains included `drums_signature` seed 11 (`+44.06`), `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+42.17`), `drums_zigzag` seed 7 (`+39.56`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`+16.64`), but losses dominated: `verse_chorus` seed 3 (`-144.29`), `dense_sprint` seed 8 (`-59.50`), `drums_pulse` seed 5 (`-48.98`), `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`-40.63`), `drums_crescendo` seed 9 (`-29.87`), `rhythm_ladder` seed 4 (`-14.22`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`-12.93`). The budget/weakness idea is sound, but changing the cache key for the combined axis-quality batch duplicated unrelated speed-stream work and starved terminal conversion.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. A future version should isolate weakness-sensitive streams from the existing cached speed streams rather than invalidating the whole axis-quality cache by incumbent quality.
