## arc-budget-weak-reuse-taper-115

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted mature extra reuse slot improves quality but has known branch churn. Instead of disabling it abruptly or gating by duplicate ratio, taper its probability smoothly by incumbent axis quality: weak passing rows keep the full accepted reuse pressure, while strong passing rows retain a 65% floor of that pressure. This should preserve the accepted mechanism where it is needed and reduce high-quality churn where it is not.
- Code changes made: Temporarily passed the current `LeafKey` best-so-far state into `rankedOptions`, `reuseCandidateLimit`, tail completion, suffix repair, and expansion. `matureReuseExtraPressure` was multiplied by `matureReuseIncumbentPressure(bestKey)`, which is `1.0` for weak incumbents, smoothly tapers between axis quality `0.36` and `0.62`, and bottoms out at `0.65`. Candidate generation formulas, engine validation, scoring, axis-quality streams, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-reuse-taper-115`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.6`; `Δheadline = -0.3`, 95% CI `[-0.9, -0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=98.1%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.4`, `200k=-0.6`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The taper removed reuse work but not the harmful kind: at 200k reuse attempts/successes fell `104963/53498 -> 103298/52707`, while selected reuse rose `2331 -> 2342`, full/unique full evaluations rose slightly `79815/55975 -> 79900/56044`, and selected axis-quality candidates fell `1693 -> 1684`. Largest 200k losses were rows that the accepted reuse breadth helped: `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-65.80`), `drums_swell` seed 11 (`-33.30`), `drums_pulse` seed 5 (`-22.75`), `drums_zigzag` seed 10 (`-9.43`), `drums_zigzag` seed 5 (`-9.14`), and `verse_chorus` seed 8 (`-4.58`). Gains were tiny: `drums_breath` seed 4 (`+4.58`), `opening_burst` seed 3 (`+1.18`), and `drums_zigzag` seed 0 (`+0.11`). The accepted reuse slot is useful even for relatively strong incumbents; incumbent-quality tapering is the wrong guard.
- Status: Reverted; not committed.

## arc-budget-weak-air-suffix-combo-116

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Two mechanisms were individually clean but too sparse: isolated next-low-air support broadened forward-air futures, and extra weak-row suffix attempts helped suffix-completable pendulum rows. Combine them as one weak/mature downstream-coverage mechanism so low-air setup candidates and a few extra suffix completions can reinforce each other without changing scoring or existing axis-quality cache behavior.
- Code changes made: Temporarily restored the isolated weak next-air `air_support` stream from attempt 114 (`lowTargetScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, weak-best threshold/width `0.62/0.30`) with a separate weak-axis-quality cache, and restored the suffix attempt-depth idea from attempt 101 by adding up to four extra suffix repair attempts through `suffixRepairBudgetPressure(targetBudget) * weakness`. Candidate geometry formulas, engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, existing axis-quality cache keys, existing speed/current-air streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-air-suffix-combo-116`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.2`; `Δheadline = +0.3`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 1.3]`, `P(Δ<=0)=22.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.5`, `200k=+0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. At 200k the combination increased air axis-quality attempts/successes `4494/1344 -> 5357/1552`, suffix attempts/successes/improvements/nodes `101/45/41/3676 -> 108/50/45/4138`, and unique full evaluations `55975 -> 56002`. Largest 200k gains were `drums_pendulum` seed 4 (`+46.12`), `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+32.10`), `drums_pendulum` seed 11 (`+26.49`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+19.83`), `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`+4.57`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`+0.46`). Regressions were `drums_pendulum` seed 9 (`-18.05`), `drums_pendulum` seed 3 (`-9.56`), and `cold_start` seed 2 (`-4.63`). The mechanism is close to acceptance, but the suffix extra-attempt ceiling reintroduced the known seed-9 loss.
- Status: Not kept as-is; revised into a lighter suffix-attempt follow-up.

## arc-budget-weak-air-light-suffix-117

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 116 was close but the +4 suffix attempt ceiling likely reintroduced the known `drums_pendulum` seed 9 loss. Keep the same isolated, budget/full-feedback/low-target/incumbent-weakness-aware next-air support stream, but make the suffix side lighter by adding at most two smooth budget/weakness-scaled attempts.
- Code changes made: Temporarily kept the isolated weak next-air `air_support` stream from attempt 114 (`lowTargetScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, weak-best threshold/width `0.62/0.30`) with its separate weak-axis-quality cache, and changed the suffix attempt-depth ceiling from four extra attempts to two extra attempts via `suffixRepairBudgetPressure(targetBudget) * weakness`. Candidate geometry formulas, engine validation, real scoring targets, ranking/scoring, existing axis-quality cache keys, existing speed/current-air streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-air-light-suffix-117`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.2`; `Δheadline = +0.3`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 1.3]`, `P(Δ<=0)=22.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.5`, `200k=+0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. At 200k the candidate added air-axis attempts/successes `4494/1344 -> 5368/1554`, suffix attempts/successes/improvements/nodes `101/45/41/3676 -> 106/50/45/4007`, unique full evaluations `55975 -> 56002`, and duplicate full evaluations fell `23840 -> 23829`. Largest 200k gains were `drums_pendulum` seed 4 (`+46.12`), `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+32.10`), `drums_pendulum` seed 11 (`+26.49`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+19.83`), `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`+4.57`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`+0.46`). Regressions remained `drums_pendulum` seed 9 (`-18.05`), `drums_pendulum` seed 3 (`-9.56`), and `cold_start` seed 2 (`-4.63`). Worst 200k rows were still `drums_pendulum` seeds with low axis quality, and extra work stayed mature-budget localized; additional budget converted only in `150k/200k` rows. The lighter suffix ceiling reduced suffix nodes versus attempt 116 but did not remove the seed-9 loss or cross the canonical acceptance gate.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The budget-aware scaling is mechanically clean and close, but this combined mechanism remains too narrow and still perturbs a few pendulum starts.

## arc-budget-cadence-catch-118

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The weak mature rows still show next-contact scarcity and low conversion from additional budget, but quality preview has previously been too expensive. Add a small semantic `cadence_catch` proposal family that is budget-aware, unique-full-feedback-aware, and smoothly activated by tight next-contact spacing. The geometry should provide a short-support, low-jitter future that lands the current contact without consuming too much downstream space.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `cadence_catch` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added one mature speed-axis stream with `samples=1`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and `cadenceScaleFrames=0.60 * FPS`, and added cadence pressure to axis-quality sample counts. The new geometry reduced contact jitter, shortened pre/post support, lowered contact/pre/post angles, and capped post-contact support as next-contact cadence tightened. Existing engine validation, scoring, contract preview behavior, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-cadence-catch-118`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 0.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=58.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.1`, `200k=+0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream was very contained and mature-budget-localized: at 200k `cadence_catch` sampled `1662` candidates with `218` direct landings (`13.1%`), `340` preclear rejections, and `987` landing failures. Total 200k axis-quality attempts/successes rose `159870/68857 -> 161399/69048`, full/unique full evaluations slightly fell `79815/55975 -> 79694/55969`, and duplicate full evaluations fell `23840 -> 23725`. Only two meaningful 200k rows changed: `drums_crescendo` seed 11 improved `+40.65`, `dense_sprint` seed 9 improved `+3.01`, and `drums_breath` seed 0 regressed `-4.83`. The concept is clean but the first geometry is too hard to land and too sparse to move canonical quality.
- Status: Not kept as-is; revised into a gentler cadence-catch geometry before deciding whether to drop the family.

## arc-budget-cadence-catch-gentle-119

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 118's scheduler was contained, but the `cadence_catch` geometry was too hard to land. Keep the same smooth budget/full-feedback/cadence-aware stream, but make the geometry less extreme: smaller angle reductions, less support shortening, lower preclear pressure, and a higher post-support floor.
- Code changes made: Same temporary `cadence_catch` mode and cadence-aware speed-axis stream as attempt 118, but softened the geometry controls: contact jitter floor `0.72 -> 0.84`, preclear pressure `0.16 -> 0.08`, speed-control cadence pressure `0.20 -> 0.10`, contact/pre/post angle offsets `-4/-4/-12 -> -2/-2/-6`, pre/post shortening `0.35/0.36 -> 0.18/0.20`, cap shortening `0.28 -> 0.14`, and cadence post floor `10 + 8*lowAir -> 14 + 8*lowAir`. Engine validation, scoring, contract preview behavior, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-cadence-catch-gentle-119`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 0.5]`, `P(Δ<=0)=55.7%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.1`, `200k=+0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The gentler geometry did not improve coverage: at 200k `cadence_catch` sampled `1667` candidates with only `202` direct landings (`12.1%`), `359` preclear rejections, and `1004` landing failures. Total 200k axis-quality attempts/successes rose `159870/68857 -> 161405/69000`, full/unique full evaluations moved to `79754/55947`, and duplicate full evaluations were essentially unchanged. The only meaningful 200k changes were still `drums_crescendo` seed 11 improving `+40.65` and `drums_breath` seed 0 regressing `-4.83`; the rest of the grid was flat. The family is too sparse and too hard to land to accept.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Cadence-aware candidate generation should not be pursued further in this short-support form.

## arc-budget-depth-aware-tail-throttle-120

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted shallow-tail throttle still spends mature quality budget on duplicate-prone `remaining=1` completions, whose best/success yield is lower than deeper tail completions and whose ordinary DFS alternative is closest. Keep the same smooth scalar-budget and unique-full-feedback pressure, but make the final-contact case receive extra deterministic throttling so additional budget flows to broader terminal exploration instead of repeated one-contact suffixes.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `QUALITY_SHALLOW_TAIL_REMAINING_ONE_EXTRA_PRESSURE = 0.45`, threaded `remainingContacts` into `shallowQualityTailThrottlePressure`, and increased only `remainingContacts <= 1` throttle as `base + (1 - base) * 0.45 * base`. Pre-validity tail completion, `remaining=2` behavior, tail window size, suffix repair, candidate generation, validation, ranking/scoring, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-depth-aware-tail-throttle-120`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.2`, `200k=-0.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The scheduler changed work in the intended direction: at 200k tail attempts/successes/improvements fell `33432/24157/1237 -> 30762/21907/1210`, duplicate full evaluations fell `23840 -> 21596`, and unique full evaluations rose `55975 -> 56142`. The `remaining=1` tail attempts fell from `12959` in the baseline analyzer to `10300` in the candidate, but the extra unique terminal feedback did not convert to score. Only one scored row changed materially: `drums_signature` seed 5 improved `+45.22` at 150k but regressed `-49.05` at 200k.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Stronger rem=1 throttling improves terminal diversity but removes a useful 200k terminal improvement on at least one row, so the accepted shallow-tail throttle should remain as-is.

## arc-budget-suffix-source-diversity-121

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Bounded suffix repair has very high best/success yield when it fires, but its two branch slots can collapse onto the same source/axis future. Within already-eligible weak/scarce suffix repair attempts, keep the fixed two-branch budget but occasionally replace the second scalar-ranked option with the best option from a different source/axis group under smooth budget and terminal-scarcity pressure.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `selectSuffixRepairOptions` for `completeBoundedSuffix`. It kept the top suffix option, then with pressure `0.55 * suffixRepairBudgetPressure(targetBudget) * (1 / (1 + (uniqueFull / 24)^2))` selected the best source/axis-diverse option for the second branch when available; otherwise it used the baseline top two. Candidate generation, validation, scoring, ordinary quality expansion, tail completion, suffix eligibility, suffix attempt/node caps, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-suffix-source-diversity-121`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.0`; `Δheadline = +0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.4, 0.8]`, `P(Δ<=0)=40.1%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.2`, `100k=-0.4`, `150k=+0.0`, `200k=+0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. At 200k the source-diverse suffix choice was efficient: suffix attempts fell `101 -> 97`, suffix successes/improvements rose `45/41 -> 48/45`, suffix nodes fell `3676 -> 3571`, and unique full evaluations rose `55975 -> 56032`. Largest 200k gains were `drums_crescendo` seed 11 (`+40.32`), `drums_tide` seed 9 (`+38.58`), `drums_swell` seed 10 (`+28.64`), `drums_crescendo` seed 4 (`+24.56`), `drums_tide` seed 5 (`+19.55`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 4 (`+9.55`). Regressions offset the 200k signal and mid-budget activation was harmful: at 50k `syncopated_switchback` seed 6 lost `-54.03`; at 100k `drums_swell` seed 10 lost `-75.85`; at 200k losses included `drums_tide` seed 8 (`-18.07`), `drums_pulse` seed 7 (`-14.68`), and `drums_crescendo` seed 8 (`-14.26`).
- Status: Not kept as-is; revised into a maturity-scaled version. The mechanism improves high-budget suffix efficiency but activates too early on rows with very little terminal evidence.

## arc-budget-suffix-mature-diversity-122

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 121 improved 200k suffix efficiency but hurt 50k/100k rows with only a handful of unique full outputs. Keep the source-diverse suffix branch idea, but multiply the smooth budget/scarcity pressure by a smooth terminal-feedback maturity term so suffix diversity appears gradually after at least some full-track evidence exists.
- Code changes made: Same temporary `selectSuffixRepairOptions` mechanism as attempt 121, but changed the pressure to `0.55 * suffixRepairBudgetPressure(targetBudget) * scarcity(uniqueFull/24) * smoothstep(uniqueFull / (uniqueFull + 8))`. Candidate generation, validation, scoring, ordinary quality expansion, tail completion, suffix eligibility, suffix attempt/node caps, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-suffix-mature-diversity-122`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 0.3]`, `P(Δ<=0)=46.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.2`, `100k=-0.0`, `150k=+0.1`, `200k=+0.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The maturity pressure removed most of attempt 121's churn but also removed most upside. At 200k only two rows changed materially: `drums_tide` seed 9 improved `+38.58`, while `drums_crescendo` seed 8 regressed `-14.26`. Aggregate 200k suffix attempts/successes/improvements/nodes moved `101/45/41/3676 -> 99/45/41/3664`, full/unique full evaluations fell `79815/55975 -> 79739/55924`, and tail attempts/successes/improvements moved `33432/24157/1237 -> 33396/24134/1239`. The smoother delay made the mechanism too weak to accept.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Suffix branch diversity is mechanically interesting but not a keeper in either sparse or mature-scaled form.

## arc-budget-current-next-low-air-123

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The isolated next-low-air support stream's cleanest avoidable regression was on a row with no current low-air stream activity. Constrain the forward-air setup stream so it appears smoothly only when both the current contact and the next contact are air-sensitive, avoiding one-off future-air perturbations while preserving sustained low-air support futures.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `lowTargetScale` and `currentLowTargetScale` to axis-quality stream policies, added a second air-axis `air_support` stream with `targetSource: "next"`, next-low-air pressure scale `0.45`, current-low-air pressure scale `0.75`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, and `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and multiplied the stream sample pressure by smooth low-target pressures for both current and next air targets. Candidate geometry, validation, scoring, existing speed/current-air streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-current-next-low-air-123`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.7`; `Δheadline = -0.2`, 95% CI `[-2.1, 0.6]`, `P(Δ<=0)=56.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.3`, `200k=-0.4`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream was active but badly allocated: at 200k air axis-quality attempts/successes rose `4494/1344 -> 5463/1630`, but full/unique full evaluations fell `79815/55975 -> 79452/55767` and tail successes fell `24157 -> 24001`. It avoided the earlier `cold_start` seed 2 loss but starved the useful pendulum/dropout gains and introduced larger regressions: `dense_sprint` seed 4 lost `-125.59`, `drums_pendulum` seed 3 lost `-9.56`, and `drums_pendulum` seed 0 lost `-5.04`; gains were `rhythm_ladder` seed 7 (`+34.83`) and `cold_start` seed 8 (`+30.42`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Current+next low-air relevance is too blunt; it spends more air work but reduces terminal conversion.

## arc-budget-preview-scarce-next-air-124

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Forward-air setup should be budget aware and downstream-aware, not globally sampled. Add a small `air_support` stream only after existing scored candidates show poor next-contact preview coverage, with sample pressure scaling smoothly by scalar budget, unique full-track feedback, low next-air target, and preview scarcity. This should preserve low-budget behavior while adding next-low-air futures where the current pool appears to leave the next contact hard to catch.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added a preview-scarce next-air setup stream inside `rankedOptions`. The stream reused existing `air_support` geometry with the next contact's air target, used deterministic fractional sampling with `samples=2`, `lowTargetScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and a smooth preview-scarcity pressure derived from existing one-contact preview survivor counts. Engine validation, candidate geometry formulas, scoring targets, ranker weights, existing streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-preview-scarce-next-air-124`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.1, 0.5]`, `P(Δ<=0)=57.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=+0.0`, `200k=+0.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The gate was active but too narrow to move canonical quality: at 200k air axis-quality attempts/successes rose `4494/1344 -> 6629/2161`, overall axis-quality attempts/successes rose `159870/68857 -> 161788/69566`, while full/unique full evaluations fell `79815/55975 -> 79541/55816`. The largest 200k improvement was `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+56.50`), with smaller `cold_start` seed 0 gain (`+0.75`); regressions were `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`-19.28`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-5.04`), and `cold_start` seed 1 (`-2.50`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Preview-scarcity gating is architecturally cleaner than unconditional next-air sampling, but this version added too much air work for one large row gain and did not pass the canonical decision gate.

## arc-budget-mature-preview-breadth-125

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Some candidates may be penalized as next-contact dead ends only because the one-contact preview samples a single next candidate. Add a smoothly budget/full-feedback-scaled second preview candidate in mature rows so the ranker can prefer current catches that leave a broader catchable next-contact future, without adding committed geometry streams.
- Code changes made: Temporarily threaded `targetBudget` into handoff candidate scoring and changed `previewFutureContacts` to use `handoffPreviewCandidateCount`, which returned `HANDOFF_PREVIEW_K` plus at most one deterministic fractional extra preview candidate. The extra preview pressure used `0.55 * smoothstep(budget/(budget+150000)) * smoothstep(uniqueFull/(uniqueFull+48))` with a node-stable dither seed. Candidate generation, validation, ranker weights, real scoring targets, existing semantic streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-mature-preview-breadth-125`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were exactly zero at every budget, with unchanged validity. Analyzer comparison showed no score or work-counter movement at any budget (`workΔ(sim=+0 cand=+0 viable=+0)` on all common-row budget deltas). The smooth preview-breadth pressure was either inactive at the relevant decision points or redundant with the existing selected futures; it did not change any track choice.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Extra preview breadth without a new committed proposal family is not a useful lever in this form.

## arc-budget-low-air-settle-126

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The existing `air_support` stream is mostly a target override, not a distinct physical future. Add a separate, smoothly budget/full-feedback/low-air-scaled `low_air_settle` geometry family that reduces contact jitter, shortens the approach, and gives low-air catches a longer supported post-contact shelf. This should create grounded low-air futures without changing existing current-air support.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `low_air_settle` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added `lowTargetScale` stream pressure support, registered one air-axis stream with `samples=1`, `lowTargetScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, and `fullFeedbackScale=48`, and added mild low-air-settle geometry controls in `targetStateControls`. Existing `air_support`, speed streams, validation, scoring targets, ranker weights, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-low-air-settle-126`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `447.8`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.8, 0.7]`, `P(Δ<=0)=71.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=-0.1`, `150k=-0.2`, `200k=-0.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The new geometry was active and physically viable: at 200k `low_air_settle` sampled `1336` candidates with `620` direct landings (`46.4%`) and zero off-beat failures; air axis-quality attempts/successes rose `4494/1344 -> 5791/1941`. It produced a large `drums_pendulum` seed 0 gain (`+71.93`) and a small `cold_start` seed 0 gain (`+1.43`), but losses dominated: `syncopated_switchback` seed 4 (`-49.83`), `drums_dropout` seed 11 (`-33.42`), `cold_start` seed 8 (`-15.81`), `cold_start` seed 1 (`-2.24`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`-1.03`). The big gain came from a weak incumbent (`axis_quality` about `0.31`), while major losses started around `0.46`, suggesting the family needs a smooth weak-incumbent gate rather than global low-air activation.
- Status: Not kept as-is; revised into weak-incumbent-gated attempt `arc-budget-weak-low-air-settle-127`.

## arc-budget-weak-low-air-settle-127

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 126's geometry is useful for genuinely weak low-air rows but harmful once the incumbent is already moderately controllable. Keep the same `low_air_settle` family, but multiply its sample pressure by a smooth incumbent-axis-quality weakness term so it fades out above about `0.40` axis quality.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `weakAxisQualityMax`/`weakAxisQualityWidth` to axis-quality stream policies, threaded the current best key into `expandNode`/`rankedOptions`, included `bestAxisQuality` in the shared axis-quality cache key, and multiplied stream sample pressure by `smoothstep((0.40 - bestAxisQuality) / 0.12)`. The geometry from attempt 126, candidate validation, scoring targets, ranker weights, existing semantic streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were otherwise unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-low-air-settle-127`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `442.5`; `Δheadline = -5.3`, 95% CI `[-8.9, -2.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Validity was unchanged, but quality fell across mature budgets: `50k=-2.3`, `100k=-8.8`, `150k=-5.3`, `200k=-5.0`. The intended low-air stream became sparse (`low_air_settle` only `218` samples at 200k), but adding best-axis quality to the shared cache key regenerated broad axis-quality work and changed unrelated speed-stream scheduling: total 200k axis-quality attempts jumped `159870 -> 216853`, speed-drag samples jumped `90753 -> 123429`, full evaluations fell `79815 -> 67687`, and terminal search churned. Large losses included `mini_burst` seed 6 (`-154.05`), `verse_chorus` seed 3 (`-144.29`), `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-94.13`), `dense_sprint` seed 3 (`-82.64`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 5 (`-75.79`), and `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`-74.09`). Gains such as `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+71.93`) were overwhelmed.
- Status: Rejected; not committed. Do not put changing incumbent quality into the shared axis-quality cache key. If weak gating is retried, isolate it to the new stream without perturbing existing cached streams.

## arc-budget-isolated-weak-low-air-settle-128

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: The weak low-air settle idea should be isolated from the shared axis-quality cache. Keep baseline cached axis-quality streams byte-stable, then add one separate `low_air_settle` candidate only when the incumbent is weak, the current air target is low, budget is mature, and terminal feedback exists.
- Code changes made: Temporarily kept the `low_air_settle` candidate mode and geometry from attempt 126, removed the shared-cache weak-key changes from attempt 127, and added `weakLowAirSettleCandidates` as a separate additive stream after cached axis-quality candidates in `rankedOptions`. Its pressure was `lowAirTarget(0.45) * smoothstep((0.40 - bestAxisQuality)/0.12) * smoothstep(budget/(budget+150000)) * smoothstep(uniqueFull/(uniqueFull+48))`, with deterministic fractional `samples=1`. Existing cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-isolated-weak-low-air-settle-128`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.0`; `Δheadline = +0.2`, 95% CI `[0.0, 1.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=57.8%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 150k and `200k=+0.4`; validity was unchanged. The isolated design avoided attempt 127's broad churn: 200k axis-quality attempts/successes moved only `159870/68857 -> 160016/68923`, full/unique full evaluations were essentially unchanged (`79815/55975 -> 79808/55974`), and `low_air_settle` sampled only `208` candidates with `87` direct landings. There were no material 200k regressions; the only material change was `drums_pendulum` seed 0 improving `+71.93`. The mechanism is clean but too narrow for canonical acceptance.
- Status: Not kept as-is; revised into a two-sample isolated weak-low-air-settle attempt to test whether the same safe gate can broaden gains.

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.

- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.1`; `Δheadline = +0.2`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 1.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=33.6%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 100k, `150k=-0.0`, and `200k=+0.6`; validity was unchanged. The stream remained contained: 200k axis-quality attempts/successes moved `159870/68857 -> 160137/68977`, full/unique full evaluations stayed effectively flat (`79815/55975 -> 79848/56011`), and `low_air_settle` sampled `445` candidates with `203` direct landings. The signal improved versus K=1 but still missed the canonical acceptance gate.

## arc-budget-isolated-weak-low-air-settle-k3-130

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 129 showed that the isolated weak low-air settle stream scales smoothly and safely with more proposal coverage. Increase the same stream to three deterministic fractional samples so weak low-air rows can see a broader set of grounded-support futures, while preserving the budget, terminal-feedback, low-air, and incumbent-weakness pressures.
- Code changes made: Same temporary isolated `weakLowAirSettleCandidates` mechanism and `low_air_settle` geometry as attempts 128-129, with `HANDOFF_LOW_AIR_SETTLE_QUALITY_K` changed from `2` to `3`. Candidate validation, scoring targets, ranker weights, baseline cached axis-quality streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.

- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = +0.6`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 2.3]`, `P(Δ<=0)=24.6%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 100k, then `150k=+0.6` and `200k=+1.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream stayed isolated and budget-mature: 200k total axis-quality attempts/successes moved `159870/68857 -> 160178/68986`, air-axis attempts/successes moved `4494/1344 -> 4978/1567`, full/unique full evaluations stayed essentially flat (`79815/55975 -> 79824/55987`), and `low_air_settle` sampled `537` candidates with `231` direct landings (`43.0%`). Largest 200k gains were `drums_pendulum` seed 8 (`+79.62`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+71.93`), and `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+42.19`), with only one material regression, `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`-1.03`). K=3 broadened the useful weak-row coverage but still missed the canonical accept threshold.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. This supports the budget-aware isolated-family architecture, but adding more samples to this one family is not enough; the next mechanism should add a structurally different future rather than further increasing `low_air_settle` K.

## arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-mature-reuse-second-97` at commit `42b2c36`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 130 nearly accepted but only added current-contact low-air settle futures. Compose that contained K=3 `low_air_settle` stream with a separate isolated next-low-air `air_support` stream so weak mature rows see two physically different future roles: grounded support at the current catch and forward setup for the next low-air contact. Both streams should scale smoothly with scalar budget, unique full-track feedback, low-air target pressure, and incumbent axis-quality weakness, without changing the shared axis-quality cache.
- Code changes made: Added `low_air_settle` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, restored the K=3 low-air-settle geometry controls from attempt 130, threaded the current best `LeafKey` into `expandNode`/`rankedOptions` as read-only `bestAxisQuality`, and added two isolated air-axis streams after the baseline cached axis-quality candidates. `weakLowAirSettleCandidates` uses current low-air pressure `0.45`, weakness threshold/width `0.40/0.12`, budget scale `150000`, full-feedback scale `48`, and three deterministic fractional samples. `weakNextAirSupportCandidates` uses next-contact low-air pressure `0.45`, weakness threshold/width `0.62/0.30`, the same budget/full-feedback scales, and one deterministic fractional `air_support` sample with next-air geometry targets. Existing cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: ACCEPT`; baseline `447.9` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = +0.6`, 95% CI `[0.0, 2.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=14.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 100k, then `150k=+0.7` and `200k=+1.2`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The composed streams remained mature-budget-localized and cache-contained: 200k axis-quality attempts/successes moved `159870/68857 -> 160766/69121`, air-axis attempts/successes moved `4494/1344 -> 5608/1716`, full/unique full evaluations stayed essentially flat (`79815/55975 -> 79765/55949`), `air_support` sampled `5073` candidates with `1487` direct landings, and `low_air_settle` sampled `535` candidates with `229` direct landings (`42.8%`). Largest 200k gains were `drums_pendulum` seed 8 (`+79.62`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`+71.93`), `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`+42.19`), and `cold_start` seed 8 (`+15.96`), with one material regression, `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`-1.03`). Worst 200k rows remain mostly `drums_pendulum`, but the weak/budget-aware composition added enough distinct future coverage to pass canonical decide.
- Status: Kept and committed. New baseline archive: `generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131/golden.json`.

## arc-budget-low-air-shelf-132

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted low-air-settle family improved only a few weak rows, while remaining `drums_pendulum` failures still have very high achieved air on low-air contacts. Add a distinct, stronger `low_air_shelf` family that uses longer, flatter post-contact support for very low-air catches, but offer it as only one isolated air-axis stream scaled smoothly by low-air target pressure, incumbent weakness, scalar budget, and unique full-track feedback.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `low_air_shelf` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added separate shelf geometry controls in `targetStateControls` that further reduced contact jitter, shortened pre-contact support, lowered contact/pre/post angles, increased post support/floor/cap, and biased post length toward the low-air ground-time target. Added `weakLowAirShelfCandidates` after the accepted `weakLowAirSettleCandidates`, with one deterministic fractional sample, low-air target scale `0.45`, weakness threshold/width `0.42/0.14`, budget scale `150000`, and full-feedback scale `48`. Existing accepted `low_air_settle` and next-air support streams, cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-low-air-shelf-132`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.3`; `Δheadline = -0.2`, 95% CI `[-1.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 150k, then `200k=-0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream was mechanically viable but harmful: at 200k `low_air_shelf` sampled `293` candidates with `133` direct landings (`45.4%`), air-axis attempts/successes rose `5608/1716 -> 6358/1960`, and full/unique full evaluations were essentially flat (`79765/55949 -> 79742/55934`). The regression was concentrated but decisive: `drums_pendulum` seed 0 lost the accepted improvement and fell `378.33 -> 301.36`, with start choice shifting from `8.61@11.0deg/r3` to `9.36@24.0deg/r0`. There were no material 200k gains to offset it.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. A stronger low-air shelf changes early branch/start selection in the wrong direction; future low-air work should avoid adding a more aggressive current-contact shelf without a stronger downstream viability guard.

## arc-budget-local-next-scarcity-air-133

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted isolated air streams are budget-aware but not locally scarcity-aware. Use already-computed one-contact preview scarcity from the existing scored candidate pool to add one extra deterministic fractional `low_air_settle` sample and one extra deterministic fractional next-air `air_support` sample only when current candidates often leave no next-contact survivor.
- Code changes made: Temporarily computed a local next-scarcity pressure inside `rankedOptions` from scored candidates' `previewSurvivors`, then threaded that pressure into `weakLowAirSettleCandidates` and `weakNextAirSupportCandidates` as an extra fractional sample term. The accepted geometry, cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-local-next-scarcity-air-133`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were exactly zero at every budget and validity was unchanged. Work counters and sample-mode counters were byte-identical against the baseline. The reason is architectural: `handoffUsesFuturePreview(qualitySearch)` returns `false`, while the isolated weak air streams only run in quality search, so the local preview-scarcity pressure was always zero in the relevant context.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Local next-contact scarcity is still a plausible allocation signal, but it cannot be consumed from the existing preview telemetry without enabling quality preview or adding a separate cheap scarcity estimator.

## arc-budget-starved-low-air-settle-134

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Some of the weakest rows have very few unique full evaluations, which suppresses the accepted `low_air_settle` stream through its full-feedback ramp. Add one extra mild `low_air_settle` sample under a smooth starved-feedback pressure that is zero with no full feedback, rises when a little full-track feedback exists but remains scarce, and fades as unique full evaluations become plentiful.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added a `starvedFullFeedbackPressure` helper and one extra deterministic fractional `low_air_settle` sample term. The pressure was `lowAirTarget(0.45) * weakAxisQuality(0.40/0.12) * maturityPressure(150000) * starvedFullFeedback(seenScale=8, scarceScale=48)`. Existing accepted `low_air_settle` and next-air support base samples, geometry, cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-starved-low-air-settle-134`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.4`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.6, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Validity was unchanged at every budget. Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-0.1`, `150k=-0.1`, and `200k=-0.1`. The stream did add work: at 200k low-air-settle samples/landings rose `535/229 -> 746/294`, air-axis attempts/successes rose `5608/1716 -> 5824/1783`, and full/unique full evaluations moved `79765/55949 -> 79806/55995`. The effect was concentrated and harmful: the only material 200k row change was `syncopated_switchback` seed 9 regressing `463.59 -> 432.06` (`-31.53`), with no offsetting material gains. The same row regressed at 100k (`-31.53`) and 150k (`-22.29`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Starved-feedback low-air sampling converts additional budget into viable candidates, but the extra futures can still perturb a working basin without improving the weakest `drums_pendulum` rows.

## arc-budget-release-vertical-current-135

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: A current candidate can land while leaving a launchy release state that makes the next low-air or tight-cadence contact harder. Reintroduce release vertical velocity metadata and add a small mature-quality ranking term for excessive absolute vertical release speed before low-air or tight next contacts, now on top of the accepted weak low-air proposal streams.
- Code changes made: Temporarily populated candidate-window velocity samples, added `releaseVelocityY` to `GapFit`, carried it through candidate evaluation and snapshot cloning, and added a release-vertical term to `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty`. The term used next low-air pressure, tight-cadence pressure, `maturityPressure(150000)`, `fullFeedbackPressure(48)`, safe vertical speed ramping from `8` to `5` px/frame, and weight `0.045`. Candidate geometry, validation gates, scoring metric, accepted isolated air streams, cached axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-release-vertical-current-135`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.9`; `Δheadline = +0.3`, 95% CI `[-0.5, 1.5]`, `P(Δ<=0)=24.8%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Validity was unchanged at every budget. Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.7`, `150k=+0.5`, and `200k=+0.2`. The mechanism increased terminal conversion at mature budgets: at 200k full/unique full evaluations rose `79765/55949 -> 80582/56523` and tail completion successes rose `24139 -> 24389`, while total axis-quality attempts fell `160766 -> 159583`. Largest 200k gains were `drums_zigzag` seed 7 (`+44.52`), `dense_sprint` seed 3 (`+21.08`), `dense_sprint` seed 5 (`+19.98`), `drums_signature` seed 1 (`+16.75`), and `drums_tide` seed 7 (`+13.57`). Losses kept the verdict inconclusive: `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`-37.50`), `drums_crescendo` seed 9 (`-29.97`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 4 (`-11.71`), `drums_crescendo` seed 10 (`-8.66`), and `drums_tide` seed 0 (`-8.57`). The signal is real and mostly mature-budget-positive, but cadence-only vertical settling can perturb some working low-air basins.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Positive but not accepted; the follow-up narrowed the pressure to next-low-air targets.

## arc-budget-low-air-release-vertical-136

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 135's broad tight-cadence component produced useful terminal conversion but also `drums_crescendo` losses. Keep the release vertical metadata, but narrow the ranking pressure so it only activates when the next contact has a low-air target; tight cadence only strengthens that low-air pressure. Use a slightly stronger weight inside this narrower scope.

Candidate geometry, validation gates, scoring metric, accepted isolated air streams, cached axis-quality streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.

- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Validity was unchanged at every budget. Per-budget deltas were effectively flat: `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.0`, and `200k=+0.0`. The narrowed pressure reduced selected-score movement to near zero and also reduced useful terminal work: at 200k full/unique full evaluations fell `79765/55949 -> 79628/55857`, tail completion successes fell `24139 -> 24095`, and no 200k row score changed materially. At 150k only `rhythm_ladder` seed 5 regressed (`-5.79`) plus a tiny `drums_signature` seed 0 movement (`-0.05`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Low-air-only vertical release setup is too weak; the useful part of attempt 135 came from broader cadence awareness, but that broader pressure is not stable enough to keep without an accepted decision.

## arc-budget-start-semantic-low-air-138

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Start feasibility lookahead still ranks starts using only normal candidates, so it may be blind to the physically distinct low-air futures that the accepted mature quality streams can later generate. Add the accepted low-air semantic families to start lookahead only, with smooth budget-aware deterministic fractional sampling, so mature budgets can prefer starts that remain workable for current or next low-air contacts.
- Code changes made: Temporarily threaded `targetBudget` through `buildStartOptions` and `startFeasibilityCost`, replaced the two start-lookahead `getCandidatesSorted` calls with `startFeasibilityCandidates`, and added one current-contact `low_air_settle` stream plus one next-contact `air_support` stream for start scoring. Both streams used `lowAirTargetPressure(target, 0.45) * maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000)` and deterministic fractional sample counts with separate seeds/attempt offsets. Actual handoff expansion, accepted isolated air streams, cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-start-semantic-low-air-138`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.8`; `Δheadline = +0.3`, 95% CI `[-1.0, 2.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=30.8%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.2`, `50k=+0.1`, `100k=-0.0`, `150k=+0.5`, and `200k=+0.4`; validity moved only at 25k (`35% -> 36%`) and was unchanged at higher budgets. The start-only streams were active: at 200k `low_air_settle` samples/direct landings rose `535/229 -> 1116/672`, `air_support` rose `5073/1487 -> 5291/1679`, and full/unique full evaluations moved `79765/55949 -> 79997/56143`, but preview zero-next counts slightly worsened (`44203 -> 44533`). Largest 200k gains were `drums_crescendo` seed 1 (`+59.46`), `drums_crescendo` seed 11 (`+51.49`), `drums_signature` seed 11 (`+39.33`), and `rhythm_ladder` seed 11 (`+32.03`). Regressions were concentrated in already-weak `drums_pendulum` starts: seed 2 fell `-49.63` with start shifting `8.61@11.0deg/r6 -> 10.61@24.0deg/r8`, and seed 1 fell `-40.13` with start shifting `9.36@24.0deg/r0 -> 8.61@24.0deg/r0`. Worst 200k rows remained dominated by `drums_pendulum`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Budget-aware semantic coverage in start lookahead can move useful basins, but start selection is high-leverage and this version lacks a strong enough downstream viability guard for `drums_pendulum`.

## arc-budget-isolated-air-preview-139

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted isolated weak-air streams add distinct futures but are scored in quality search with future preview disabled. Keep generation unchanged, but score only those isolated air candidates with the existing one-contact preview probe so their selection becomes forward-aware without enabling full quality preview for the normal, brake, reuse, or cached axis-quality pools.
- Code changes made: Temporarily changed the isolated `weakLowAirSettleCandidates`/`weakNextAirSupportCandidates` scoring path so `scoreCandidateForHandoff` used `preview || axisQualitySearch` for those candidates only. Candidate generation, smooth budget/full-feedback/weakness pressures, geometry, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, start options, cached axis-quality streams, reuse, brake streams, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were otherwise unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-isolated-air-preview-139`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `447.9`; `Δheadline = -0.7`, 95% CI `[-2.4, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=98.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 100k, then `150k=-0.7` and `200k=-1.2`; validity was unchanged. The preview work was not huge but was directionally harmful: at 200k previews/contacts moved `63013/18810 -> 63544/18960`, preview zero-next worsened `44203 -> 44584`, full/unique full evaluations stayed flat (`79765/55949 -> 79768/55949`), and selected air-axis-quality candidates fell (`air:16 -> air:10`). Low-air-settle sampling rose `535/229 -> 842/355`, but it selected away from accepted gains. Largest 200k regressions were `drums_pendulum` seed 8 (`-79.62`, start `10.61@24.0deg/r9 -> 9.36@11.0deg/r0`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-76.97`, start `8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`), `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`-42.19`), and `cold_start` seed 8 (`-15.96`). The only material 200k improvement was `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`+1.03`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The existing one-contact normal-candidate preview is not a safe validator for the isolated weak-air family; it can penalize the physically useful low-air futures before the broader quality search can exploit them.

## arc-budget-speed-carry-140

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Current speed proposal families are asymmetric: there are several physical braking/drag futures for overspeed, but no corresponding carry-speed future for underspeed. Add a budget-mature `speed_carry` semantic family that activates under smooth underspeed pressure, exits more downhill with a longer support, and lets ranking/search choose it only when preserving speed improves the current gap.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `speed_carry` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added `underspeedScale` support to `AxisQualityStreamPolicy`, and registered one current-speed axis-quality stream with `samples=1`, `underspeedScale=0.45`, `budgetScaleFrames=150000`, and `fullFeedbackScale=48`. In `targetStateControls`, `speed_carry` used `smoothstep(underspeed)` to raise contact/pre/post angles and modestly increase pre/post support length/cap. Existing accepted low-air streams, cached overspeed streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, start options, reuse, brake streams, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were otherwise unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-speed-carry-140`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.3`; `Δheadline = -0.2`, 95% CI `[-1.2, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 100k, then `150k=-0.1` and `200k=-0.5`; validity was unchanged. The stream was not physically viable enough: at 200k `speed_carry` sampled `3434` candidates but only directly landed `27` (`0.8%`), mostly failing landing validation (`1201` landing failures). Speed axis-quality attempts rose `155158 -> 158495`, while full/unique full evaluations fell slightly (`79765/55949 -> 79699/55899`). The largest 200k regression was `verse_chorus` seed 3 (`-144.29`, start `10.61@10.5deg/r7 -> 8.61@-15.5deg/r0`); there were no material 200k gains. Worst rows remained dominated by `drums_pendulum`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Underspeed/carry-speed remains a plausible missing physical role, but this downhill/long-support geometry is too hard to land and should not be retried without a substantially different landing strategy.

## arc-budget-speed-adaptive-low-air-settle-141

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Detailed `drums_pendulum` reports show early low-air contacts overshooting speed and later low-air contacts becoming underspeed. Instead of adding a separate hard-to-land carry stream, keep the accepted `low_air_settle` stream and support geometry, but separate its support role from its braking role: fade only the extra low-air-settle angle/brake offsets when the target-state rider is already underspeed.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added smooth `underspeed = max(speedError, 0)` inside `targetStateControls`, derived `lowAirSettleBrakePressure = lowAirSettlePressure * (1 - 0.65 * smoothstep(underspeed))`, and used that brake pressure for low-air-settle contact/pre/post angle offsets and post-angle minimum. Contact jitter, preclear pressure, post support length/floor/cap, candidate streams, budget/full-feedback/weakness pressures, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, start options, reuse, brake streams, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were otherwise unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-speed-adaptive-low-air-settle-141`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.0`; `Δheadline = -0.5`, 95% CI `[-2.3, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through 100k, then `150k=-0.5` and `200k=-1.0`; validity was unchanged. The mechanism increased low-air-settle activity but harmed selection: at 200k `low_air_settle` samples/direct landings rose `535/229 -> 827/330`, air-axis attempts/successes rose `5608/1716 -> 5992/1830`, and full/unique full evaluations were nearly flat (`79765/55949 -> 79776/55963`). The accepted gains were lost for `drums_pendulum` seed 8 (`-79.62`, start `10.61@24.0deg/r9 -> 9.36@11.0deg/r0`) and seed 0 (`-76.97`, start `8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`); there were no material 200k gains.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The accepted low-air-settle braking angles appear to be part of the useful basin; simply fading them under underspeed reproduces the same start/branch loss seen in stronger shelf and preview attempts.

## arc-budget-next-low-air-settle-142

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted next-air stream uses `air_support`, which mostly changes the geometry target and leaves the support-settling role to the current-contact stream. Add a separate isolated next-low-air `low_air_settle` stream so the candidate pool includes a grounded-support setup future for the next low-air contact, with the same smooth budget, feedback, low-air target, and incumbent-weakness pressures as the accepted next-air stream.

Accepted current `low_air_settle`, accepted next `air_support`, cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.

- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.3`; `Δheadline = -0.3`, 95% CI `[-1.3, 0.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=82.1%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=-0.1`, `150k=+0.0`, and `200k=-0.6`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream added substantial low-air-settle coverage but did not improve terminal conversion: at 200k `low_air_settle` samples/direct landings rose `535/229 -> 1495/579`, `air_support` moved `5073/1487 -> 5264/1478`, air-axis-quality attempts/successes rose `5608/1716 -> 6759/2057`, and full/unique full evaluations fell slightly (`79765/55949 -> 79648/55908`). Largest 200k regressions were `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-76.97`, start `8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`) and seed 9 (`-39.82`, start `10.61@11.0deg/r6 -> 9.36@11.0deg/r0`); the largest gain was `drums_dropout` seed 7 (`+18.70`, start `10.97@1.0deg/r7 -> 10.97@-25.0deg/r8`). Worst 200k rows stayed concentrated in `drums_pendulum`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The budget-aware scaling was mechanically sound, but this next-contact settle family is not sufficiently distinct from the accepted current low-air settle role and perturbs the fragile `drums_pendulum` start/branch basin.

## arc-budget-high-air-release-143

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Baseline `drums_pendulum` failure shape shows opposite air/speed errors across the phrase: low-air sections are over-air and underspeed, while high-air sections are under-air and overspeed. Add a physically distinct mature-budget high-air release family that shortens post-contact support and steepens the exit for high-air targets, gated by smooth high-air target pressure, incumbent axis-quality weakness, scalar budget, and terminal feedback.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `high_air_release` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added high-air-release geometry controls in `targetStateControls` that slightly widened contact jitter, raised preclearance, steepened contact/post angle, shortened pre/post support, and lowered the post-support floor/cap for clean release. Added one isolated air-axis `weakHighAirReleaseCandidates` stream before the accepted weak low-air streams, with one deterministic fractional sample, high-air target scale `0.45`, weakness threshold/width `0.46/0.18`, budget scale `150000`, and full-feedback scale `48`. Accepted low-air streams, cached axis-quality streams, scoring targets, ranker weights, validation, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-high-air-release-143`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Validity and per-budget scores were effectively unchanged, with only a tiny 200k row movement (`drums_crosscut` seed 10 `-0.02`). The stream was active but not viable: at 200k `high_air_release` sampled `672` candidates across `81` rows, but only `7` directly landed (`1.0%`), with `114` preclear rejects and `551` direct failures, mostly landing failures (`539`). Air-axis-quality attempts rose `5608 -> 6265`, but selected air-axis-quality candidates stayed flat (`16 -> 16`), and full/unique full evaluations were essentially unchanged (`79765/55949 -> 79750/55941`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The budget-aware allocation shape was safe, but the proposed high-air release geometry did not produce physically viable catches. A future high-air role needs a landing-first geometry strategy, not a steeper/shorter release fragment.

## arc-budget-release-air-setup-144

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Instead of adding another hard-to-land geometry family, use existing engine-validated release metadata to make quality ranking forward-aware in a smooth, budget-aware way. Mature-budget candidates that set up low-air targets should prefer sustained grounded release, while candidates before high-air targets should prefer cleaner airborne release, with the engine still deciding viability.
- Code changes made: Temporarily extended `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty` with `releaseAirSetupPenalty`, using existing `releaseGroundedFrames` and `releaseAirborne` candidate metadata. The new term was active only in quality search and scaled by `maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000) * fullFeedbackPressure(48)`. It used current/next low-air pressure and next high-air pressure to penalize low-air candidates with too little grounded release and high-air-next candidates with too much grounded release or non-airborne release. Existing candidate generation, accepted low-air streams, cached axis-quality streams, validation gates, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-release-air-setup-144`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `447.8`; `Δheadline = -0.7`, 95% CI `[-2.9, 1.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=76.5%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-0.8`, `150k=-0.4`, and `200k=-1.1`; validity was unchanged. The term moved real mature selections but not safely: at 200k `low_air_settle` samples/direct landings rose `535/229 -> 754/324`, `air_support` rose `5073/1487 -> 5319/1506`, air-axis-quality successes rose `1716 -> 1830`, but selected air-axis-quality candidates stayed flat (`16 -> 16`) and full/unique full evaluations fell slightly (`79765/55949 -> 79673/55910`). Largest 200k losses were `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-92.83`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-76.97`, start `8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`), `drums_swell` seed 2 (`-70.06`), `dense_sprint` seed 4 (`-65.16`), `drums_pendulum` seed 10 (`-51.97`), and `drums_breath` seed 6 (`-51.34`). Largest gains were `drums_tide` seed 2 (`+54.81`), `drums_dropout` seed 7 (`+50.01`), `drums_tide` seed 4 (`+46.80`), and `dense_sprint` seed 8 (`+44.47`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Existing release-grounded/airborne metadata is a useful signal, but this direct ranking pressure is too broad and perturbs working branches. Future forward-awareness should probably be candidate-family-specific or used diagnostically to allocate proposal coverage, not as a global quality score term.

## arc-budget-air-axis-release-vertical-145

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 144 showed that broad release-state scoring is too destabilizing, while attempt 135 showed release vertical velocity can improve terminal conversion. Add release vertical velocity metadata but apply the mature-budget vertical-settle penalty only to air-axis quality candidates, so forward-awareness affects the semantic air streams without re-ranking the normal pool, reuse, or brake candidates.
- Code changes made: Temporarily populated `Detection.measurements.velocity` in the fast window and raw candidate detection paths, added `releaseVelocityY` to `GapFit`, carried it through candidate evaluation, handoff branch cloning, and polish cloning, and added `airAxisReleaseVerticalPenalty` to `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty`. The penalty was active only when `sourceAxis === "air"`, scaled by next-low-air/tight-cadence pressure, `maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000)`, and `fullFeedbackPressure(48)`, with safe absolute vertical speed ramping from `8` to `5` px/frame. Candidate generation, accepted low-air streams, cached axis-quality streams, validation gates, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-air-axis-release-vertical-145`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = +0.0`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: The canonical curve, validity, worst rows, and paired row scores were unchanged at every budget. The scoped release-vertical term did not alter selected branches; no material regressions or improvements appeared.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Air-axis-only release vertical setup is too narrow to affect the search. The useful part of release-vertical awareness likely needs broader candidate selection, but attempts 135 and 144 show that broad release-state ranking needs a better guard before it is safe.

Worst rows remained concentrated in `drums_pendulum`.
