## arc-budget-weak-release-vertical-149

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 135 showed that release vertical velocity can improve mature terminal conversion but was too broad. Add release vertical velocity metadata again, but apply a smaller release-vertical setup penalty only in mature quality search when the incumbent full result has weak axis quality, and scale it smoothly by current/next low-air pressure, tight next-contact cadence, budget maturity, and full-feedback pressure.
- Code changes made: Temporarily populated candidate-window velocity samples, added `releaseVelocityY` to `GapFit`, preserved it through candidate evaluation, handoff cloning, and polish cloning, and extended `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty` with a guarded release-vertical term. The term used smooth weak-quality, low-air/cadence, budget, and full-feedback pressures, a safe vertical-speed ramp from `8` to `5` px/frame, and weight `0.032`. Candidate generation, accepted low-air streams, cached axis-quality streams, validation gates, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-release-vertical-149`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.6`; `Δheadline = +0.1`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.3]`, `P(Δ<=0)=58.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through `100k`, then `150k=+0.1` and `200k=+0.1`; validity was unchanged. The effect was extremely narrow: at 200k full/unique full evaluations moved `79765/55949 -> 79825/56016`, tail completion successes moved `24139 -> 24134`, selected axis-quality candidates moved `1695 -> 1694`, selected air-axis candidates stayed `16 -> 16`, and selected speed-axis candidates moved `1679 -> 1678`. The only material 200k row movement was `drums_tide` seed 7 improving `+13.57`; no material 200k regressions appeared in the analyzer output.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The guard made release-vertical setup safe but too weak to produce an accepted canonical signal. Forward release-state awareness remains plausible, but it needs to affect candidate-state coverage more directly than this scoring nudge.

## arc-budget-suffix-extra-attempts-150

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Bounded suffix repair has very high improvement yield but a fixed global attempt cap. Let weak mature rows receive up to two extra suffix-repair attempts through the existing smooth budget and axis-weakness pressure, so additional budget can convert into complete downstream futures without changing candidate geometry, ranking, or low-budget behavior.
- Code changes made: Temporarily replaced the fixed `QUALITY_SUFFIX_REPAIR_MAX_ATTEMPTS` gate with `suffixRepairAttemptLimit(targetBudget, weakness)`, returning the base cap of `6` plus up to `2` extra attempts via `suffixRepairBudgetPressure(targetBudget) * weakness`. Candidate generation, validation, ranking/scoring, accepted low-air streams, cached axis-quality streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail scheduling, suffix branching/nodes, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-suffix-extra-attempts-150`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.7`; `Δheadline = +0.2`, 95% CI `[-0.0, 1.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=52.2%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through `100k`, then `150k=+0.4` and `200k=+0.2`; validity was unchanged. The suffix channel expanded slightly and stayed high-yield: at 200k suffix attempts/successes/improvements/nodes moved `103/46/42/3747 -> 107/49/45/3984`, while full/unique full evaluations stayed essentially flat (`79765/55949 -> 79758/55951`). The largest 200k gain was the intended `drums_pendulum` seed 4 suffix completion (`+46.12`, start `10.61@24.0deg/r9 -> 8.61@24.0deg/r6`), plus a tiny `drums_pendulum` seed 7 gain (`+0.46`); the offsetting loss was `drums_pendulum` seed 9 (`-18.05`). Worst rows remained `drums_pendulum`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Smooth extra suffix attempts are mechanically clean but too narrow and still trade one pendulum basin against another. More suffix depth is not enough by itself.

## arc-budget-starved-suffix-extra-151

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 150's suffix depth helped a scarce-terminal `drums_pendulum` row but regressed a row that already had more unique full evaluations. Keep the same smooth weak/mature extra suffix attempts, but multiply the extra-attempt pressure by a smooth unique-full scarcity term so extra suffix work remains available for starved weak rows and fades out once terminal diversity exists.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `suffixRepairAttemptScarcityPressure(uniqueFull) = 1 - smoothstep(uniqueFull / (uniqueFull + 12))` and used it in the dynamic suffix attempt limit. The attempt limit remained the base `6` plus up to `2` extra attempts scaled by suffix budget pressure, weakness pressure, and the new full-feedback scarcity pressure. Candidate generation, validation, ranking/scoring, accepted low-air streams, cached axis-quality streams, reuse, brake streams, start options, tail scheduling, suffix branching/nodes, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-starved-suffix-extra-151`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.7`; `Δheadline = +0.2`, 95% CI `[0.0, 1.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=58.4%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through `100k`, then `150k=+0.3` and `200k=+0.3`; validity was unchanged. The scarcity guard removed the attempt-150 `drums_pendulum` seed 9 loss while preserving the seed 4 gain. At 200k suffix attempts/successes/improvements/nodes moved `103/46/42/3747 -> 104/47/43/3808`, full/unique full evaluations stayed flat (`79765/55949 -> 79766/55950`), and the only material 200k score movement was `drums_pendulum` seed 4 improving `+46.12`. No material 200k regressions appeared.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The budget/weakness/scarcity scaling is clean, but one-row movement is still too narrow for canonical acceptance. This is useful evidence for suffix scheduling, not a keepable mechanism.

## arc-budget-air-support-geometry-152

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: The existing `air_support` stream is budget-aware through the quality-stream allocation but has no distinct placement geometry, so it mostly behaves like another normal sample aimed at a low-air target. Give only `air_support` a mild grounded-support role: slightly tighter contact placement, modestly shorter approach, a little more post-contact support, and a small settled/uphill exit bias. This should broaden physically distinct low-air setup futures without adding samples, changing branch counts, or introducing fixed budget thresholds.
- Code changes made: Added a smooth `airSupportPressure` in `targetStateControls` for `mode === "air_support"` and applied mild contact-jitter, preclear, speed-control, angle, pre-length, post-length, safe-cap, and post-floor adjustments. Normal, brake, startup, speed-drag, and accepted `low_air_settle` streams, stream scheduling, ranking/scoring, validation, start options, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-air-support-geometry-152`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `446.9`; `Δheadline = -1.6`, 95% CI `[-4.5, -0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=99.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=-2.5`, `150k=-1.0`, and `200k=-2.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The geometry made `air_support` more landable but not more useful: 200k air-support samples/direct landings moved `5073/1487 -> 5624/1857`, air-axis attempts/successes moved `5608/1716 -> 6417/2166`, but selected air-axis candidates only moved `16 -> 18`, selected speed-axis candidates fell `1679 -> 1674`, and full/unique full evaluations fell `79765/55949 -> 79501/55730`. Largest 200k regressions were `syncopated_switchback` seed 9 (`-85.11`), `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-76.97`, start `8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 5 (`-60.05`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 3 (`-43.37`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 7 (`-37.73`). Largest gains were `syncopated_switchback` seed 8 (`+40.50`), `cold_start` seed 10 (`+11.60`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 11 (`+10.26`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 9 (`+5.98`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. A mild `air_support` physical role increased landing coverage, but it displaced speed/setup futures and destabilized fragile start/branch basins. Future support work should not broadly alter the existing `air_support` geometry without a more selective downstream-state guard.

## arc-budget-weak-air-branch-preserve-153

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted weak air streams generate physically distinct low-air futures, but selected air-axis branches remain rare. Instead of adding more candidates or changing geometry, preserve one already-validated air-axis candidate in the branch set only for weak mature low-air rows. The pressure scales smoothly by current/next low-air target, incumbent axis-quality weakness, scalar budget, and unique full-track feedback, and replaces only the third branch when the scalar top three have no air-axis candidate.
- Code changes made: Added `preserveWeakAirBranch()` to `rankedOptions()`. In quality search only, when `bestAxisQuality` is available, it may replace the third sorted branch with the best `axisq`/`air` option. The deterministic activation pressure is `lowAirTarget * weakAxisQuality * maturityPressure(budget, 150000) * fullFeedbackPressure(48)` with a node-stable hash. Candidate generation, candidate geometry, validation, scoring terms, branch count, start options, reuse/brake streams, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-budget-weak-air-branch-preserve-153`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `448.5`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.4, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=100.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through `100k`, then `150k=+0.4` and `200k=-0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The selector was very narrow and mostly a no-op: at 200k selected axis-quality candidates moved `1695 -> 1693`, selected air-axis candidates moved `16 -> 15`, full/unique full evaluations moved `79765/55949 -> 79803/55998`, and work counters were effectively flat. The only material 200k row change was harmful: `drums_pendulum` seed 0 lost `-76.97` with the start changing `8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`. No material gains appeared in analyzer output.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Preserving a single air branch after scalar ranking does not reliably increase selected air futures and can still perturb a fragile start basin. Future diversity work needs either a better downstream-state guard or a proposal family that improves selected futures without branch replacement.

## arc-low-air-candidate-lookahead-154

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Low-air targets on tight/first gaps are partly controlled by grounded support after the requested contact, but candidate evaluation currently measures those short gaps only through the contact frame unless the post-contact span is large. This can rank catches against mostly pre-contact airborne time that the current geometry cannot physically change. Extend local candidate air measurement by a smooth fraction of the post-contact interval before the next required contact, scaled by how low the air target is, so low-air support candidates are judged by controllable grounded time while the final golden scorer remains unchanged.
- Code changes made: Added `LOW_AIR_LOOKAHEAD_TARGET_SCALE = 0.45` and changed `axisLookaheadEndFrame()` so short low-air gaps extend measurement by `round(postContactFrames * smoothstep((0.45 - targetAir) / 0.45))`. Existing long-gap/full-next-contact lookahead remains unchanged. Candidate generation, placement geometry, ranking weights, branch counts, start options, axis-quality streams, tail/suffix scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-low-air-candidate-lookahead-154`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `435.5`; `Δheadline = -13.0`, 95% CI `[-41.8, 0.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=97.0%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.1`, `50k=-8.3`, `100k=-23.7`, `150k=-4.3`, and `200k=-16.9`; validity moved `25k 35%->36%`, `50k 97%->96%`, `100k 100%->98%`, `150k 100%->100%`, and `200k 100%->100%`. The change caused a 200k pass-to-fail in `drums_pendulum` seed 5 (`353.51 -> 0.01`, start `9.36@50.0deg/r7 -> 8.61@11.0deg/r0`) and large 200k regressions in `drums_pendulum` seed 6 (`-115.10`), seed 8 (`-88.50`), seed 3 (`-74.12`), and seed 7 (`-60.20`), plus `dense_sprint` seed 0 (`-82.21`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`-74.09`), and `opening_burst` seed 8 (`-60.62`). Largest 200k gains were `dense_sprint` seed 1 (`+59.53`), `dense_sprint` seed 5 (`+55.92`), `drums_crescendo` seed 11 (`+53.16`), `drums_crescendo` seed 1 (`+48.05`), and `rhythm_ladder` seed 3 (`+47.03`), but these were overwhelmed by start-basin churn in `drums_pendulum`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Smooth candidate-side low-air lookahead made candidate ranking chase a different short-gap basin instead of improving controllable downstream support. This reinforces that budget/cadence scaling needs a generator that adds durable physical futures, not a broader local air measurement on first/tight gaps.

## arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155

- Baseline used: `arc-budget-weak-air-settle-combo-131` at commit `43aa823`.
- Hypothesis: Broad release-vertical setup previously increased full/unique terminal conversion but was too global, while scarce suffix repair produced a narrow high-yield improvement. Combine these two smooth mature-budget mechanisms: expose release vertical velocity from the already-simulated candidate window and penalize launchy exits only in quality search before low-air or tight next contacts, while allowing up to two extra suffix-repair attempts only for weak rows whose unique full feedback is still scarce.
- Code changes made: Added `releaseVelocityY` to `GapFit`, populated candidate-window velocity samples in the fast and raw candidate detectors, preserved the field through handoff and polish cloning, and added release-vertical coverage diagnostics to compile stats. Extended `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty()` with a release-vertical term using next low-air pressure, tight-cadence pressure, `maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000)`, `fullFeedbackPressure(48)`, safe absolute vertical speed ramping from `8` to `5` px/frame, and weight `0.045`. Replaced the fixed suffix attempt cap with a base `6` plus up to `2` extra attempts scaled by suffix budget pressure, axis-quality weakness, and a smooth scarcity fade from unique full evaluations. Candidate generation, placement geometry, validation gates, scoring metric, accepted isolated air streams, cached axis-quality streams, start options, tail scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: ACCEPT`; baseline `448.5` -> candidate `449.0`; `Δheadline = +0.5`, 95% CI `[-0.5, 1.8]`, `P(Δ<=0)=15.6%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.8`, `150k=+0.7`, and `200k=+0.5`; validity was unchanged at every budget. Terminal diversity improved at 200k (`full/unique full 79765/55949 -> 80679/56697`), tail completions rose (`24139 -> 24314`), suffix attempts/successes/improvements moved `103/46/42 -> 104/48/43`, and selected axis-quality candidates fell slightly (`1695 -> 1683`, air `16 -> 15`, speed `1679 -> 1668`). Largest 200k improvements were `drums_pendulum` seed 4 (`+46.12`, start `10.61@24.0deg/r9 -> 8.61@24.0deg/r6`), `drums_zigzag` seed 7 (`+44.52`), `opening_burst` seed 10 (`+34.03`), `drums_swell` seed 9 (`+24.91`), `dense_sprint` seed 3 (`+21.08`), and `dense_sprint` seed 5 (`+19.98`). Largest 200k regressions were `dense_sprint` seed 7 (`-51.20`, start `12.24@-12.0deg/r2 -> 12.24@1.0deg/r5`), `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`-37.50`), `drums_crescendo` seed 9 (`-29.97`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`-14.39`), `drums_crescendo` seed 10 (`-8.66`), and `drums_tide` seed 0 (`-8.57`).
- Status: Kept and committed. New baseline archive: `generated/golden-runs/arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155/golden.json`.

## arc-scarce-suffix-branch-156

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted suffix repair path remains scarce but high-yield. Some weak `drums_pendulum` rows still have few unique full outputs, and extra attempts alone only helped one row. Let bounded suffix completion take one additional branch only for mature, weak, unique-full-scarce suffix attempts, so additional budget can expose physically different downstream completions without changing normal candidate generation, contract search, or full-track scoring.
- Code changes made: Added `QUALITY_SUFFIX_REPAIR_EXTRA_BRANCHES = 1`, threaded a per-attempt suffix branch limit into `completeBoundedSuffix()`, and set the limit to base `2` plus a deterministic fractional extra branch using suffix budget pressure, incumbent axis-quality weakness, and the existing unique-full scarcity pressure. Candidate generation, placement geometry, release-vertical scoring, validation gates, branch count outside suffix repair, start options, tail scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-scarce-suffix-branch-156`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `449.0`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.5, 0.4]`, `P(Δ<=0)=72.7%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through `100k`, then `150k=-0.1` and `200k=-0.1`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The suffix channel did more work and had higher local yield at 200k (`suffix 48/104 -> 52/98`, improvements `43 -> 45`), but total terminal diversity fell (`full/unique full 80679/56697 -> 80507/56563`) and tail completions fell (`24314 -> 24277`). The largest 200k gain was `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+63.51`), with small gains in `drums_tide` seed 6 (`+4.18`) and `drums_crosscut` seed 11 (`+0.17`). Regressions outweighed them: `drums_crescendo` seed 8 (`-46.35`), `drums_tide` seed 8 (`-19.92`), `drums_pulse` seed 7 (`-7.89`), `drums_pendulum` seed 10 (`-5.93`), and `drums_dropout` seed 7 (`-5.70`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Extra suffix branching exposes different completions, but the added branch swaps between fragile suffix basins instead of providing stable aggregate improvement.

## arc-release-vertical-weak-guard-157

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted release-vertical setup improves weak rows but still perturbs some working basins. Keep the same release-state metadata and mature budget/full-feedback scaling, but multiply only the release-vertical term by a smooth incumbent axis-quality weakness pressure so the vertical settling effect concentrates on rows whose current full result is still weak.
- Code changes made: Added `HANDOFF_RELEASE_VERTICAL_WEAK_AXIS_QUALITY = 0.58` and width `0.28`, threaded `bestAxisQuality` through `scoreCandidateForHandoff()` into `candidateReleaseSetupPenalty()`, and multiplied `releaseVerticalSetupPressure()` by `smoothstep((0.58 - bestAxisQuality) / 0.28)`. The release-speed setup term, suffix repair, candidate generation, placement geometry, validation gates, start options, tail scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-release-vertical-weak-guard-157`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `448.9`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-0.6, 0.3]`, `P(Δ<=0)=72.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.1`, `150k=-0.1`, and `200k=-0.3`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The guard recovered some accepted regressions, notably `drums_crescendo` seed 7 (`+37.50`) and `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`+14.39`) versus the 155 baseline, but it removed larger accepted gains: `dense_sprint` seed 3 (`-42.95`), `drums_swell` seed 9 (`-24.91`), `drums_crosscut` seed 9 (`-18.20`), `drums_signature` seed 1 (`-16.75`), and `opening_burst` seed 10 (`-13.41`). Terminal diversity fell at 200k (`full/unique full 80679/56697 -> 80283/56418`) and tail completions fell (`24314 -> 24205`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Incumbent-quality guarding is too blunt: the accepted release-vertical signal helps medium-quality rows that look superficially safe, so fading it by aggregate axis quality removes more useful futures than harmful ones.

## arc-weak-scarce-tail-window-158

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Remaining weak rows often still have low unique full-track feedback even after the accepted release-vertical/suffix change. Let near-tail completion start up to two contacts earlier only when the incumbent full result is weak, budget is mature, and unique full feedback is scarce. This should expose complete downstream futures from existing engine-validated candidates without changing normal generation, suffix repair, or scoring.
- Code changes made: Added `TAIL_COMPLETION_WEAK_SCARCE_WINDOW_EXTRA = 2` and `TAIL_COMPLETION_WEAK_SCARCE_FULL_EVALUATIONS = 24`, passed the current best key into `completeNearTail()`/`shouldAttemptNearTailCompletion()`, and added `weakScarceTailCompletionWindowExtra()` to extend the near-tail window by `2 * budgetPressure * axisQualityWeakness * uniqueFullScarcity`. Suffix repair still uses the accepted baseline tail window, and candidate generation, placement geometry, release-vertical scoring, validation gates, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid are unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-weak-scarce-tail-window-158`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `449.1`; `Δheadline = +0.1`, 95% CI `[0.0, 0.6]`, `P(Δ<=0)=58.6%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: The effect was concentrated at `100k`: per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.6`, `150k=+0.0`, and `200k=+0.0`; validity was unchanged at every budget. At 200k row scores were unchanged, but the tail completer did exercise the added depth: tail successes/improvements moved `24314/1230 -> 24318/1231`, with a new `rem=10` tail bucket (`11` improvements from `12` successes and `38` attempts). Terminal diversity was essentially flat (`80679/56697 -> 80664/56679` full/unique full).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The smooth extra tail window is directionally plausible at 100k but does not meet the canonical acceptance gate against the 12-seed baseline, and it adds no 200k quality.

## arc-release-settle-family-159

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted release-vertical setup only ranks against launchy exits; it does not add a physical future that intentionally settles the rider before a tight or next-low-air contact. Add one isolated, budget/full-feedback-scaled `release_settle` stream in quality search, activated by the same smooth next-low-air/tight-cadence pressure, so higher budgets can see a distinct supported-exit candidate without perturbing the normal prefix or shared cached streams.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `release_settle` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`; added mode-specific geometry controls that reduced contact jitter, shortened the approach, lowered/settled post-contact angles, and modestly increased supported post-contact length within the existing downstream cap; and added an isolated release-settle stream after the accepted isolated air streams. The stream used one deterministic fractional sample scaled by `releaseVerticalSetupPressure()` and used next-air geometry targets when present. Normal candidate generation, cached axis-quality streams, release-vertical ranking, suffix/tail scheduling, validation gates, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-release-settle-family-159`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `448.4`; `Δheadline = -0.7`, 95% CI `[-2.6, 1.1]`, `P(Δ<=0)=77.7%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=-1.6`, `150k=-0.8`, and `200k=-0.4`; validity was unchanged at every budget. The stream was active but too costly and low-yield: at 200k `release_settle` sampled `15218` candidates with `3899` direct landings (`25.6%`), air-axis attempts/successes jumped `5633/1740 -> 20991/5612`, total axis-quality attempts/successes rose `159481/68941 -> 174317/72617`, but full/unique full evaluations fell `80679/56697 -> 78765/55628` and tail successes fell `24314 -> 23639`. Largest 200k gains were `opening_burst` seed 0 (`+84.13`), `syncopated_switchback` seed 11 (`+83.49`), `opening_burst` seed 5 (`+54.99`), `rhythm_ladder` seed 7 (`+42.43`), and `drums_dropout` seed 9 (`+40.94`). Regressions outweighed them: `drums_pendulum` seed 8 (`-56.60`), `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-52.76`), `drums_pulse` seed 5 (`-48.98`), `drums_breath` seed 4 (`-42.95`), `dense_sprint` seed 9 (`-38.87`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-37.55`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. A dedicated supported-exit proposal family can produce large row wins, but this broad activation starves terminal feedback and displaces better speed/setup futures. Future release-shape work needs a stronger scarcity/downstream-state guard or lower-cost landability before adding thousands of air-axis samples.

## arc-next-air-release-settle-160

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 159's broad extra stream was too expensive, but the settled-release shape may still be useful if it replaces an existing low-volume stream instead of adding work. Keep the accepted weak next-air support stream's smooth budget, full-feedback, next-low-air, and incumbent-weakness gates and sample count, but make that stream emit `release_settle` geometry rather than the mostly normal `air_support` geometry.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `release_settle` to `CANDIDATE_SAMPLE_MODES`, added the same settled-release geometry controls from attempt 159, and changed only `weakNextAirSupportCandidates()` to sample with `mode: "release_settle"` while retaining its existing geometry targets, sample count, seed, and smooth gate. No new stream, extra samples, scoring change, suffix/tail scheduling change, validation gate change, start option change, golden spec change, evaluator fingerprint change, seed-set change, metric change, or budget-grid change was made.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-next-air-release-settle-160`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `448.9`; `Δheadline = -0.1`, 95% CI `[-1.0, 0.2]`, `P(Δ<=0)=57.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=+0.0`, `100k=+0.0`, `150k=-0.0`, and `200k=-0.3`; validity was unchanged at every budget. Work stayed contained compared with attempt 159: 200k release-settle sampled only `676` candidates with `255` direct landings (`37.7%`), air-axis attempts/successes moved `5633/1740 -> 5935/1909`, and full/unique full evaluations were nearly flat (`80679/56697 -> 80606/56640`). However, the same-cost replacement still produced a fragile start-basin loss: `drums_pendulum` seed 0 regressed `-76.97` (`start 8.61@11.0deg/r3 -> 9.36@24.0deg/r0`). Gains were narrow: `cold_start` seed 10 improved `+32.67` and `drums_dropout` seed 8 improved `+11.03`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The same-cost version avoided terminal starvation, but replacing next-air support geometry is not robust: one large `drums_pendulum` regression outweighs narrow wins. Future release-shape work should avoid touching start-fragile first/tight basins or should require a direct downstream-state scarcity signal rather than next-air pressure alone.

## arc-smooth-shallow-tail-throttle-161

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Current 200k tail diagnostics still show low-yield duplicate-prone completions at shallow remaining depths, while `remaining=8..9` is much higher yield. Extend the existing quality-phase shallow-tail throttle from a hard `remaining<=2` guard to a smooth remaining-contact pressure, still scaled by budget maturity and full-feedback, so mature budgets spend less on shallow duplicate completions and more on ordinary quality exploration.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `QUALITY_SHALLOW_TAIL_THROTTLE_REMAINING_SCALE = 5`, removed the `remaining <= 2` condition around `shouldKeepShallowQualityTailCompletion()`, and multiplied the existing budget/full-feedback throttle by `smoothstep((scale - remainingContacts) / scale)`. Candidate generation, candidate geometry, ranking/scoring, accepted air/speed streams, release-vertical setup, suffix repair, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-smooth-shallow-tail-throttle-161`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `448.8`; `Δheadline = -0.2`, 95% CI `[-1.2, 0.0]`, `P(Δ<=0)=91.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were zero through `150k` and `200k=-0.6`; validity was unchanged. The smooth remaining-depth pressure weakened the existing strongest shallow throttle rather than freeing useful work: 200k tail successes rose `24314 -> 26185`, duplicate full evaluations rose `23982 -> 25843`, unique full evaluations were nearly flat/slightly lower (`56697 -> 56615`), and tail best/success rate fell `5.1% -> 4.8%`. The paired loss was concentrated: `verse_chorus` seed 3 regressed `-144.29` (`start 10.61@10.5deg/r7 -> 8.61@-15.5deg/r0`) and `rhythm_ladder` seed 4 regressed `-14.22`; there were no material improvements (`drums_zigzag` seed 0 was only `+0.11`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. The current shallow-tail throttle is already doing useful duplicate control at `remaining<=2`; smoothing it without preserving full shallow pressure makes the scheduler worse. Future tail work should preserve the existing shallow suppression and only add carefully targeted deeper-depth suppression if diagnostics justify it.

## arc-strong-shallow-tail-throttle-162

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 161 showed the existing `remaining<=2` shallow-tail throttle is useful, but mature 200k diagnostics still have duplicate-prone shallow tail completions. Preserve the original shallow scope and smooth budget/full-feedback scaling, but raise the maximum throttle pressure from `1.0` to `1.6` so high-budget rows with enough full feedback suppress more duplicate shallow completions without touching candidate geometry, ranking, or deeper tail completion.

Candidate generation, candidate geometry, release-vertical setup, suffix repair, scoring, validation gates, start options, deeper tail scheduling, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.

- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `449.0`; `Delta headline = -0.0`, 95% CI `[-0.2, 0.0]`, `P(Delta<=0)=70.2%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were unchanged through `100k`, then `150k=+0.1` and `200k=-0.2`; validity was unchanged. The stronger throttle did reduce duplicate full evaluations at 200k (`23982 -> 14958`) and improved the unique-rate (`70.3% -> 79.2%`), but it also cut full evaluations (`80679 -> 72021`) and tail successes (`24314 -> 15232`) too aggressively. The only material 200k regression was `drums_signature` seed 5 (`-49.05`, start `9.53@-2.5deg/r7 -> 8.28@-2.5deg/r9`), while improvements were negligible (`drums_swell` seed 9 `+0.30`, `drums_zigzag` seed 0 `+0.11`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Duplicate suppression alone is not a structural quality source: budget-aware throttles should free work only when there is a replacement stream that adds distinct validated downstream states, otherwise they can remove useful completion feedback.

## arc-speed-carry-family-163

The family did expose some useful speed futures (`drums_crescendo` seed 9 `+65.58`, `drums_crescendo` seed 7 `+37.50`, `drums_pendulum` seed 5 `+31.33`), but it landed too poorly to justify the added stream: at 200k `speed_carry` sampled `5736` candidates with only `273` direct landings (`4.8%`), while speed-axis attempts rose `154336 -> 159323` and full/unique full fell slightly (`80679/56697 -> 80408/56530`). Regressions included a large `verse_chorus` seed 3 basin loss (`-144.29`) and `syncopated_switchback` seed 5 (`-16.32`).

## arc-release-state-branch-diversity-164

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Candidate pools may already contain physically distinct futures that scalar ranking collapses away. In mature quality search, when the incumbent axis quality is still weak, preserve the top two scalar branches but let the third branch prefer an already engine-validated candidate whose release speed, release vertical velocity, grounded frames, or airborne state differs from those top branches. This should improve downstream-state coverage without adding samples, preview work, new geometry, or scorer changes, and the pressure scales smoothly with budget, full-track feedback, and incumbent weakness.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added a quality-phase `selectReleaseStateDiverseOptions()` selector after scalar sorting in `rankedOptions()`. It computed a release-state novelty score from existing candidate metadata and adjusted only the third branch by `1.2 * weakAxisQualityPressure * maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000) * fullFeedbackPressure(48)`. Candidate generation, placement geometry, release-vertical scoring, suffix repair, validation gates, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-release-state-branch-diversity-164`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: INCONCLUSIVE`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `448.4`; `Delta headline = -0.6`, 95% CI `[-2.4, 1.4]`, `P(Delta<=0)=77.7%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.1`, `100k=-1.6`, `150k=-0.7`, and `200k=-0.2`; validity was unchanged. The selector produced broad branch churn rather than durable state coverage: at 200k full/unique full evaluations fell (`80679/56697 -> 79550/55982`), selected axis-quality candidates were essentially flat (`1683 -> 1687`; air `15 -> 12`, speed `1668 -> 1675`), and candidate counts barely changed. Largest 200k gains included `dense_sprint` seed 7 (`+66.80`), `opening_burst` seed 7 (`+63.12`), `dense_sprint` seed 5 (`+59.72`), `drums_dropout` seed 11 (`+54.00`), and `drums_crosscut` seed 9 (`+52.66`), but broad regressions outweighed them: `drums_dropout` seed 2 (`-51.48`), `drums_breath` seed 4 (`-49.44`), `opening_burst` seed 8 (`-49.05`), `grain_staircase` seed 0 (`-37.49`), and `drums_pendulum` seed 0 (`-34.55`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Release-state branch novelty is too blunt as a selector: it moves start/frontier routing before the search can prove the alternate state is actually better downstream. Future state coverage should be added by candidate generators or by a stricter downstream viability signal, not by generic branch novelty.

## arc-scarce-next-low-air-settle-165

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Broad next-low-air and release-settle streams previously added useful futures but spent too much work and perturbed fragile basins. The stream used existing `low_air_settle` geometry with the next contact's air target, one deterministic fractional sample, next-low-air pressure `0.45`, weak-axis pressure `0.50/0.20`, `maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000)`, and a smooth starved-full-feedback pressure with seen/scarce scales `8/48`. Candidate scoring, release-vertical setup, suffix repair, validation gates, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-scarce-next-low-air-settle-165`

The stream added low-air-settle coverage but did not convert it into selected quality: at 200k `low_air_settle` samples/direct landings rose `535/230 -> 1124/411`, air-axis attempts/successes rose `5633/1740 -> 6327/1929`, selected air-axis candidates moved only `15 -> 16`, and full/unique full stayed slightly lower (`80679/56697 -> 80446/56540`). Largest 200k regression was `dense_sprint` seed 11 (`-99.94`, start `12.24@-12.0deg/r3 -> 13.49@14.0deg/r2`), followed by `syncopated_switchback` seed 1 (`-40.28`) and `drums_tide` seed 6 (`-17.58`); the only material gain was `syncopated_switchback` seed 8 (`+5.89`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Even a starved-feedback next-low-air settle stream spends air work without creating durable downstream improvement. Low-air support futures need a different landing/setup mechanism rather than more low-air-settle samples.

## arc-duplicate-semantic-reserve-166

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: The accepted scarce/mature semantic reserve makes quality proposal allocation budget-aware, but it treats mature diverse terminal feedback and mature duplicate-heavy terminal feedback the same. Add a small smooth duplicate-full pressure to the existing mature reserve so rows that are repeatedly producing duplicate full outputs trade a little more normal candidate depth for existing semantic streams, while low-budget and early-feedback behavior stays unchanged.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `axisQualityDuplicateReservePressure()`, using `0.25 * maturityPressure(targetBudget, 150000) * fullFeedbackPressure(uniqueFull, 48) * smoothstep(duplicateFull / (duplicateFull + 32))`, and combined it additively with the existing mature reserve before the `max()` against the scarce-feedback reserve. Candidate generation, candidate geometry, scoring, validation gates, release-vertical setup, suffix repair, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.

- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were unchanged through `100k`, then `150k=-0.5` and `200k=-0.4`; validity was unchanged. The reserve shifted mature work only slightly: at 200k full/unique full were essentially flat/slightly higher (`80679/56697 -> 80666/56773`), duplicate full stayed flat (`23982 -> 23893`), and selected axis-quality candidates were nearly unchanged (`1683 -> 1685`). The quality movement was worse: the largest 200k regression was `dense_sprint` seed 0 (`-95.92`, start `12.24@1.0deg/r6 -> 13.49@-12.0deg/r9`), while gains were tiny (`opening_burst` seed 6 `+2.61`, `drums_crosscut` seed 9 `+1.83`, `opening_burst` seed 7 `+0.79`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Duplicate-aware reservation is too weak a proxy for useful alternate futures; it can perturb a mature basin without creating better downstream state coverage.

## arc-adaptive-next-speed-bias-171

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Worst rows alternate between overspeed and near-zero speed after speed-control streams. The existing next-speed settle/deep-settle policies use fixed below-target geometry biases (`-0.16`, `-0.30`) whenever sampled. Keep the same sample scheduling and scoring, but make only the physical geometry target bias proportional to smooth current overspeed pressure, so mild overspeed gets a less aggressive setup while severe overspeed still gets the full braking future.
- Code changes made: Temporarily added `axisQualityPolicyGeometryTarget()`. `axisQualityCandidates()` still computed sample counts from the existing scheduled target, but next-source speed policies with `targetBias` and `overspeedScale` generated geometry using `baseNextTarget + targetBias * axisQualityStreamPressure(baseNextTarget)`. Candidate sample counts, stream registration, scoring, validation gates, release-vertical setup, suffix/tail scheduling, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-adaptive-next-speed-bias-171`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `444.2`; `Delta headline = -4.8`, 95% CI `[-11.1, 1.8]`, `P(Delta<=0)=93.1%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=-0.0`, `50k=+0.8`, `100k=-7.2`, `150k=-3.7`, and `200k=-6.5`; validity was unchanged. Terminal diversity improved (`full/unique full 80679/56697 -> 81700/57760`) and suffix local yield improved (`48/104 -> 53/100`, suffix best `43 -> 50`), but row routing got worse: selected speed-axis candidates fell (`1668 -> 1566`) while start/branch churn dominated. Largest 200k regressions were `dense_sprint seed0 -116.84`, `drums_dropout seed2 -106.58`, `drums_swell seed7 -100.54`, `syncopated_switchback seed5 -91.16`, and `syncopated_switchback seed1 -86.71`; gains included `opening_burst seed0 +100.30`, `verse_chorus seed5 +85.86`, and `drums_crescendo seed11 +84.28`.
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Adaptive next-speed geometry has leverage and can increase terminal diversity, but applying it to all biased next-speed streams changes too many mature branches. A future version needs a direct downstream-state scarcity or row-stability guard rather than changing the default physical target for every sampled biased speed setup candidate.

## arc-suffix-smooth-speed-bias-172

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Attempt 171 showed adaptive next-speed geometry has leverage but destabilizes ordinary quality routing. Restrict the adaptive geometry target bias to bounded suffix repair, and make its strength a smooth pressure from suffix budget maturity, incumbent weakness, and scarce unique full feedback, so low-budget or less-scarce repairs stay close to baseline while mature weak scarce repairs can explore a different downstream speed setup.
- Code changes made: Temporarily changed the axis-quality cache key to include an adaptive speed-bias pressure, threaded that numeric pressure through `rankedOptions()` and suffix repair only, and generated next-source speed geometry as `baseNextTarget + targetBias * overspeedPressure * suffixRepairPressure` for biased speed policies. Main quality expansion, contract search, tail completion, sample counts, stream registration, scoring, validation gates, release-vertical setup, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-suffix-smooth-speed-bias-172`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: REJECT`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `448.2`; `Delta headline = -0.9`, 95% CI `[-2.7, 1.0]`, `P(Delta<=0)=85.3%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+0.0`, `50k=-0.0`, `100k=-0.3`, `150k=-0.7`, and `200k=-1.6`; validity was unchanged. Suffix local yield improved (`48/104 -> 54/99`) and suffix best moved `43 -> 47`, but terminal feedback diversity fell (`full/unique full 80679/56697 -> 79782/56046`) and tail best fell (`1230/24314 -> 1212/24073`). Largest 200k regressions were `drums_zigzag` seed 2 (`-69.39`), `drums_swell` seed 4 (`-65.68`), `drums_breath` seed 10 (`-61.44`), `drums_dropout` seed 10 (`-57.55`), and `drums_dropout` seed 5 (`-54.85`); gains included `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+63.51`), `drums_crescendo` seed 6 (`+35.34`), `drums_tide` seed 6 (`+25.52`), and `drums_dropout` seed 7 (`+21.48`).
- Status: Reverted; not committed. Smooth budget awareness made the adaptive bias less destructive than attempt 171, but still swapped fragile suffix/frontier basins and reduced terminal diversity. The lesson is that smooth scaling is necessary but not sufficient: the physical proposal also needs to preserve or increase broad full-run feedback rather than only improving suffix-local conversion.

## arc-contract-low-budget-tail-window-173

- Baseline used: `arc-release-vertical-scarce-suffix-155` at commit `890b216`.
- Hypothesis: Many low-budget failures are deep partial contract runs with zero full evaluations rather than bad full-track quality. Let contract-phase near-tail completion start earlier only when budget is scarce, using a smooth low-budget pressure and the existing bounded two-wide suffix completer, so 25k/50k runs can convert deep viable prefixes into full outputs before quality search begins. Once a full contract exists, keep the existing quality tail window unchanged.
- Code changes made: Added `CONTRACT_TAIL_COMPLETION_LOW_BUDGET_WINDOW_EXTRA = 14` and `CONTRACT_TAIL_COMPLETION_LOW_BUDGET_SCALE_FRAMES = 75000`, and changed `tailCompletionContactWindow()` to take `qualitySearch`. Contract-phase calls now use `8 + 14 * (1 - smoothstep(budget / (budget + 75000)))`; quality-phase calls keep the previous `8 + 2 * smoothstep(budget / (budget + 150000))`. Candidate generation, candidate geometry, ranking/scoring, validation gates, release-vertical setup, suffix repair, start options, golden specs, evaluator fingerprint, seed set, metric, and budget grid were unchanged.
- Golden command: `LR_ENGINE=wasm npm run golden -- --jobs=32 --archive-dir=generated/golden-runs/arc-contract-low-budget-tail-window-173`
- Decide result: `VERDICT: ACCEPT`; baseline `449.0` -> candidate `451.5`; `Delta headline = +2.5`, 95% CI `[-3.4, 7.9]`, `P(Delta<=0)=15.9%`.
- Notable regressions/improvements: Per-budget deltas were `25k=+57.6`, `50k=+8.0`, `100k=+0.6`, `150k=-1.4`, and `200k=-2.0`; validity moved `25k 35% -> 70%` and was unchanged at higher budgets. Terminal diversity at 200k was roughly flat/slightly higher (`full/unique full 80679/56697 -> 80942/56707`), tail completions rose (`24314 -> 24561`), and suffix attempts/successes/improvements fell slightly (`104/48/43 -> 100/45/40`). Largest 200k regressions were `verse_chorus` seed 3 (`-109.82`), `dense_sprint` seed 4 (`-96.72`), `drums_swell` seed 7 (`-94.13`), `drums_signature` seed 5 (`-49.05`), and `syncopated_switchback` seed 2 (`-43.79`); gains included `drums_crescendo` seed 3 (`+63.51`), `drums_swell` seed 10 (`+28.64`), `drums_dropout` seed 8 (`+26.81`), `drums_breath` seed 2 (`+22.57`), and `drums_crescendo` seed 9 (`+20.65`).
- Status: Kept and committed. New baseline archive: `generated/golden-runs/arc-contract-low-budget-tail-window-173/golden.json`.
